L479: ## The Applicant's Position:
L480: The efficacy and safety evaluation of tebentafusp for the proposed indication is based primarily on data from the ongoing pivotal, Phase 3 Study 202 and ongoing supportive, Phase 1/2 Study 102 that evaluated the proposed dosing regimen of tebentafusp in HLA-A*02:01-positive patients with mUM in the 1L and 2L+ settings, respectively. The remaining 3 studies contributed safety data only. Studies included in the safety dataset are presented in Section 8.2 of this document.
L481: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L482: FDA agrees with the Applicant's summary of clinical trials relevant to the application. For this BLA application, the clinical data for FDA's analysis of efficacy and safety were based on data from Study 202.
L483: ## 8 Statistical and Clinical Evaluation
L484: ## 8.1. Review of Relevant Individual Trials Used to Support Efficacy
L485: ## 8.1.1. Pivotal Study 202
L486: ## Trial Design
L487: ## The Applicant's Description:
L488: ## Overall design
L489: This is an ongoing open-label, randomized, multi-center study to evaluate the efficacy and safety of tebentafusp versus investigator's choice (dacarbazine, ipilimumab, or pembrolizumab) in adult HLA-A*02:01-positive patients with mUM who had not received prior systemic therapy in the metastatic setting. Enrolled patients were randomized 2:1 to receive either tebentafusp (Arm1) or investigator's choice (Arm 2), the latter of which was selected prior to randomization. Randomization was stratified by LDH status. The study schema is depicted in Figure 25.
L490: **Arm 1 (tebentafusp):** All patients randomized to Arm 1 received tebentafusp by intravenous (IV) infusion following the intra-patient escalation regimen. Patients received 20 mcg on Day 1, 30 mcg on Day 8, 68 mcg on Day 15, and 68 mcg once every week thereafter. Due to the anticipated cytokine release-associated toxicity with tebentafusp following the first 3 doses, patients were monitored for at least 16 hours after dosing as an inpatient following the weekly doses on Days 1, 8, and 15.
L491: **Arm 2 (investigator's choice):** All patients randomized to Arm 2 received investigator's choice of one of the following 3 options: dacarbazine at the standard dosing regimen in UM of 1000 mg/m2 given on Day 1 of each 21-day cycle; ipilimumab at the dosing regimen for unresectable or metastatic melanoma of 3 mg/kg given on Day 1 of each 21-day cycle for a maximum of 4 doses; or pembrolizumab at the dosing regimen of 2 mg/kg up to a maximum of 200 mg or 200 mg administered IV where approved locally given on Day 1 of each 21-day cycle. The preferred investigator's choice agent was selected prior to randomization. No extended monitoring after dosing was required in Arm 2.
L492: Figure 25 Study 202 Design
L493: C#D# = Cycle # Day #; HLA = human leukocyte antigen; LDH = lactate dehydrogenase; Q3W = every 3 weeks; RP2D-IE = recommended Phase 2 dose intra-patient escalation regimen; ULN = upper limit of normal.
L494: Treatment continued until radiographic progression (except for patients receiving ipilimumab), unacceptable toxicity, investigator decision, or patient withdrawal of consent. Patients receiving tebentafusp, pembrolizumab, or ipilimumab experiencing disease progression per RECIST could continue with treatment beyond initial RECIST progression if they met the prespecified criteria.
L495: ## **Choice of control group**
L496: Prior to this study, no therapy has demonstrated a survival benefit in UM (Ramaiya, 2007; Augsberger, 2009; Khattak, 2013). In the absence of standard of care, options for UM patients have included PD-1 inhibitors, ipilimumab, or chemotherapy. Therefore, the comparator arm for this randomized study was an investigator's choice with 3 options that included pembrolizumab, ipilimumab, and dacarbazine.
L497: ## **Eligibility criteria**
L498: Key inclusion criteria included:
L499: - \ge 18 years of age at the time of informed consent.
- Histologically or cytologically confirmed mUM.
- No prior systemic therapy in the metastatic or advanced setting.
- No regional liver-directed therapy.
- HLA-A*02:01 positive by central assay.
- ECOG performance status score of 0 or 1.
L500: Key exclusion criteria included:
L501: - Systemic or untreated CNS metastases.
- History of severe hypersensitivity reactions to other iologic drugs or mAbs.
- Clinically significant cardiac or impaired cardiac function.
- Active infection that required systemic antibiotic therapy.
- Known HIV.
L502: - Active hepatitis B virus or hepatitis C virus infection.
- History of adrenal insufficiency.
- History of interstitial lung disease.
- History of pneumonitis requiring corticosteroid treatment or current pneumonitis.
- History of colitis or inflammatory bowel disease.
- Treatment with systemic steroid therapy or any other immunosuppressive medication at any dose level.
- Radiotherapy within 2 weeks of first dose of study drug.
- Use of hematopoietic colony-stimulating growth factors.
L503: ## Dose selection
L504: ## *Tebentafusp*
L505: The dosing regimen of tebentafusp used in Study 202 was 20 mcg on Day 1, 30 mcg on Day 8, 68 mcg on Day 15, and 68 mcg once every week thereafter. Tebentafusp is administered via IV infusion over 15 to 20 minutes. The selection of intra-patient dose escalation over the first 3 weeks was identified in FIH, Phase 1 Study 01 and the actual doses were selected during the Phase 1 portion of Study 102. The final, recommended dosing regimen was then studied in the Phase 2 portion of Study 102 and Study 202.
L506: Several key observations in Study 01 led to recommending an intra-patient escalation regimen using fixed doses. First, in Study 01, patients with higher body weights who received higher absolute doses of tebentafusp experienced more severe treatment-related toxicity. Consequently, fixed doses, rather than weight-based doses, were incorporated during the study. Second, given no clinically meaningful differences in safety or efficacy were identified between the weekly and daily dosing regimens, and in light of the easier utility of a weekly regimen, subsequent evaluation focused on weekly dosing. Third, the emerging safety profile of tebentafusp suggested that CRS (and associated hypotension) was more likely to occur during the first 2 weeks, after which tachyphylaxis occurred. Therefore, as has been used for other T cell-engaging bispecifics, an intra-patient escalation regimen during Weeks 1 and 2 (ie, 20 and 30 mcg, respectively) prior to dosing 50 mcg was selected as the preferred regimen to evaluate further in the subsequent single-arm, Phase 1/2 Study 102.
L507: In Study 102, the intra-patient escalation regimen based on fixed dosing identified in Study 01 was further optimized by evaluating higher Week 3 doses (54 to 73 mcg) to achieve greater exposure. During the Phase 1 portion of this study, which employed a standard 3+3 dose-escalation design, the 73 mcg dose level was not tolerated due to elevations in hepatic transaminases with concurrent low-grade increases in bilirubin in 2 of 4 treated patients. These hepatic events were reversible without administration of corticosteroids, and all patients who experienced dose-limiting toxicities remained on treatment. After a dose reduction to 68 mcg, 6 patients were treated with no dose-limiting toxicities and no significant liver enzyme elevations. As a result, a dose of 68 mcg was identified as the maximum tolerated dose and selected for further evaluation as part of the intra-patient escalation regimen during the Phase 2 portion of Study 102 and was subsequently also incorporated into Study 202.
L508: ## *Investigator's choice*
L509: The investigator's choice options (pembrolizumab, ipilimumab, or dacarbazine) were administered at approved doses and regimens for the treatment of metastatic melanoma.
L510: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L511: FDA agrees with the Applicant's summary of the pivotal trial, Study 202. The comparator arm consisting of Investigator's choice of dacarbazine, ipilimumab or pembrolizumab is considered appropriate and consistent with current standard of care for the systemic treatment of unresectable and metastatic uveal melanoma.
L512: ## Study Endpoints
L513: ## The Applicant's Description:
L514: ## **Primary endpoint**
L515: The primary endpoint of Study 202 was OS, defined as the time from randomization until death by any cause. The endpoint of OS had dual primary objectives relating to: (1) OS in all patients randomized to tebentafusp versus all patients randomized to investigator's choice (ITT Analysis Set); and (2) OS in all patients randomized to tebentafusp who developed a rash during the first week of treatment versus all patients randomized to investigator's choice (RAS).
L516: ## **Secondary endpoints**
L517: Secondary endpoints were:
L518: - Safety and tolerability (AEs, SAE, laboratory parameters, vital signs, ECGs)
- PK (serum concentrations over time)
- PFS
- Tumor response (BOR, DOR, TTR, DCR [defined as CR or PR, or SD \ge24 weeks])
- HRQoL (assessed using EQ-5D, 5L and EORTC QLQ-C30)
L519: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L520: FDA agrees with the applicant's summary of study endpoints. It should be noted that although OS in the RAS analysis population was one of the primary objectives in this study, FDA's risk:benefit assessment of tebentafusp was only based on the OS in all patients randomized (ITT Analysis population).
L521: ## Statistical Analysis Plan and Amendments
L522: ## The Applicant's Description:
L523: ## **Analysis populations**
L524: Four analysis sets were used in this study, as defined in Table 14.
L525: **Table 14: Study 202: Analysis Populations**
L526: Analysis Set	Definition
ITT Analysis Set	The ITT Analysis Set comprised all patients assigned to treatment analyzed by the treatment assignment whether or not the patient received the assigned treatment. All patients randomized in the study were analyzed in the ITT population. The ITT Analysis Set was used for all summaries and analyses of demography, baseline characteristics, disposition, medical history, prior anticancer therapy, and efficacy data summaries and analyses.
RAS	The RAS comprised all patients assigned to tebentafusp who developed a rash within the first week of treatment (ie, Study Days 1 to 7 and prior to the second dose in case the second dose was received early) and all patients randomized to investigator's choice regardless of rash. If the analysis for the RAS crossed the pre-specified stopping boundaries and stopping boundaries for the ITT Analysis Set at the same planned analysis were not crossed, then this analysis set was also to be used for demography, baseline characteristics, efficacy, and safety data summaries and analyses. If the stopping boundaries for the RAS OS analysis were crossed, then summaries of demographics, baseline characteristics, and safety were also to be run on this analysis set.
Safety Analysis Set	The Safety Analysis Set included all randomized patients who received at least 1 full or partial dose of tebentafusp or investigator's choice. Patients were classified in this set according to initial treatment received. The Safety Analysis Set was used for all safety summaries.
PK Analysis Set	The PK Analysis Set included patients in the Safety Analysis Set who had at least 1 measurable PK concentration and who had the relevant date, time, and dosing data for the sample.
L527: ITT = Intent-to-treat; OS = overall survival; PK = pharmacokinetics; RAS = Rash Analysis Set.
L528: ## **Multiple testing strategy**
L529: To provide strong control of the type I error rate, the primary endpoint of OS and key secondary endpoints of PFS and BOR were to be tested in this sequential order for the ITT analyses. If any previous analysis in the sequence was not statistically significant, the alpha could not be transferred to subsequent analyses. Since neither PFS nor BOR were tested on an interim basis, both endpoints were tested at an overall alpha level of 0.045 (2-sided) or 0.05 if the alpha from the RAS analysis was transferred to the ITT analyses.
L530: The final analysis of PFS was to be based on 274 progression and death events. Assuming a median PFS of 5 months in the tebentafusp arm and 3.3 months in the investigator's choice arm, an HR of 0.66, and the same accrual and drop-out assumptions described earlier for OS, the analysis of PFS was expected to have 90% power to demonstrate a difference in the survival distributions between the 2 arms. Due to the higher hazard of progression events relative to death events, the required number of PFS events was expected to occur prior to the first interim analysis of OS. If the required number of events did not occur at the time of the first OS interim analysis, then the PFS analysis was to occur at a later time when 274 progression events had occurred.
L531: The analysis of BOR was to occur after all 369 randomized patients were followed for approximately 9 months (ie, at least 3 planned on-treatment assessments) for response. It was to be formally tested for statistical significance only if the null hypotheses for OS and PFS in the ITT Analysis Set were both rejected.
L532: ## **Interim analyses**
L533: Two interim analyses were to be performed using a 3-stage group sequential design. Separate analyses were to be performed for each of the 2 primary objectives, but the analyses were to occur at the same time and the timing would be driven by the number of events in the ITT Analysis Set. The first interim analysis was based on approximately 60% of the events (150 events). The second interim analysis was to be based on approximately 80% of the events (200 events). Analyses of OS were based on O'Brien-Fleming boundaries (O'Brien, 1979). The Lan-DeMets approach (Lan, 1983) that approximates the O'Brien-Fleming spending function was used to adjust for situations where the actual number of events up to the DCO date for a given interim analysis did not match the planned number.
L534: For the first interim analysis, the RAS analysis was conducted first. If the stopping boundaries were crossed, then the full alpha for that analysis (\alpha = 0.005) was added to the alpha allocated for the ITT analyses (\alpha = 0.045), resulting in a total \alpha_{ITT} = 0.05. If, at a particular interim analysis, stopping boundaries were crossed for the RAS analysis but not the ITT analysis, then the ITT alpha allocation for future analyses was adjusted, as described by Maurer and Bretz (Maurer, 2013). Therefore, stopping boundaries for the ITT analyses were adjusted accordingly. Secondary endpoints such as PFS and BOR were not formally tested in the RAS and were not tested on an interim basis.

L542: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L543: FDA agrees with the Applicant's summary of the statistical analysis plan of Study 202. In addition to what is included in the Applicant's summary, additional details on sample size determination and analysis methods for the primary and key secondary endpoints are provided below.
L544: ## **Analysis Methods:**
L545: The difference in OS between the treatment arms was tested using a 2-sided log-rank test stratified by LDH status (LDH &gt; ULN versus LDH ≤ ULN; measured centrally). The OS HR was estimated using a stratified Cox-proportional hazards model using the Efron approach for handling ties, along with corresponding 95% CIs for the HR. The analysis method for PFS was the same as that of OS. ORR was compared between treatment arms using a stratified Cochran Mantel-Haenszel (CMH) test adjusting for baseline LDH status.
L546: ## **Sample Size:**
L547: The sample size in Study 202 was driven by OS in the all randomized population. Assuming a 2:1 randomization ratio of tebentafusp versus investigator's choice, 250 events (deaths) were required to provide 89% power to detect an OS HR of 0.645 at 2-sided significance level of 0.045. Assuming OS was exponentially distributed and a median OS of 12 months in the investigator's choice arm, the HR of 0.645 corresponds to a median OS of 18.6 months in the tebentafusp arm resulting in a difference of 6.6 months in median OS between the arms. Considering a non-uniform recruitment of about 33 months and 10% annual drop-out rate, 369 patients were to be randomized in a 2:1 ratio to the 2 arms to observe 250 events after 51 months as follows. This sample size accounts for two formal interim analyses of OS.
L548: ## **Protocol Amendments**
L549: ## **The Applicant's Description:**

L577: ## Patient Disposition
L578: ## Data:
L579: From 04 October 2017 to 18 June 2020, 447 HLA-A*02:01-positive patients were screened, of whom 378 patients were randomly assigned (2:1) to tebentafusp (n = 252) or investigator's choice (n = 126; Figure 26) at 58 sites in 14 countries (US, Germany, France, United Kingdom, Poland, Canada, Australia, Belgium, Spain, Switzerland, Ukraine, Russia, Italy, and the Netherlands). Treatment choices available in the investigator's choice arm were pembrolizumab (n = 103; 81.8%), ipilimumab (n = 16; 12.7%), and dacarbazine (n = 7; 5.6%).
L580: As of 13 October 2020, the DCO date of the primary analysis, the median duration of follow-up for all patients was 14.1 months (range, 12.7 to 15.6 months).
L581: A total of 245 patients (97.2%) in the tebentafusp arm and 111 patients (88.1%) in the investigator's choice arm received at least 1 dose of study drug (Figure 26). Seven patients (2.8%) in the tebentafusp arm and 15 patients (11.9%) in the investigator's choice arm were randomized but not treated, with the main reason being patient decision (1.6% and 11.1%, respectively). Of the patients who were randomized but not treated, 3 patients (42.9%) in the tebentafusp arm and 10 patients (66.7%) in the investigator's choice arm continued to be followed for survival at the DCO. At least 6 of 10 patients (60.0%) in the investigator's choice arm received anti-PD-L1/cytotoxic T lymphocyte-associated antigen-4 (CTLA-4) therapy, of whom 5 patients received anti-PD-L1/CTLA-4 combination therapy.
L582: A total of 172 patients (68.3%) in the tebentafusp arm and 100 patients (79.4%) in the investigator's choice arm had discontinued treatment, while 73 patients (29.0%) continued to receive tebentafusp and 11 patients (8.7%) continued to receive investigator's choice (Figure 26). The most common reason for permanent discontinuation of study drug was PD, which occurred in 154 patients (61.1%) in the tebentafusp arm and 78 patients (61.9%) in the investigator's choice arm.
L583: A total of 83 patients (32.9%) in the tebentafusp arm and 41 patients (32.5%) in the investigator's choice arm discontinued study treatment but remained on study (Figure 26). Study discontinuation was reported for 96 patients (38.1%) in the tebentafusp arm and 69 patients (54.8%) in the investigator's choice arm, with the main reason for discontinuation being death (87 and 63 patients, respectively).
L584: Figure 26 Study 202: Patient Disposition (ITT Analysis Set)
L585: Alt = alternative; Invest = investigator; IP = investigational product; ITT = Intent-to-treat; PD = progressive disease; tx = therapy.
L586: a 20/30/68 mcg refers to the dosing regimen applied throughout the study. The proposed dosing regimen is 20 mcg at Cycle 1 Day 1, 30 mcg at Cycle 1 Day 8, and 68 mcg at Cycle 1 Day 15 and weekly thereafter.
L587: Source: Module 5.3.5.1, Study 202 CSR, Table 14.1.1 and Table 14.1.2.
L588: ## The Applicant's Position:
L589: The causes of study treatment discontinuation were generally consistent with the disease under study.
L590: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L591: FDA agrees with the Applicant's summary of patient disposition based on the data cutoff date of 13 October 2020. Ninety-four percent of randomized participants received study treatment. Proportionally, more patients on the Investigator's choice arm discontinued study treatment than on the tebentafusp arm. The majority of treatment discontinuations on both arms were
L592: due to disease progression. Overall, the breakdown and percentages of participant disposition in the study were acceptable.
L593: ## Protocol Violations/Deviations
L594: ## Data:
L595: At least 1 type of important protocol deviation was reported in 99 patients (39.3%) in the tebentafusp arm and 36 patients (28.6%) in the investigator's choice arm (Table 17). The most common (&gt; 10% of patients) important protocol deviations in both treatment arms were related to trial procedures (22.6% and 11.1%, respectively).

L600: ## The Applicant's Position:
L601: Overall, the higher rate of protocol deviations in the tebentafusp arm compared with the investigator's choice arm is related to more frequent dosing (weekly vs every 3 weeks) and an intensive schedule of assessments and visits in the tebentafusp arm. Given the number of patients enrolled and the volume of data collected over the study duration, the deviations were determined to not impact the interpretation of the study results as they pertain to the study's relevant objectives and endpoints. Moreover, the COVID-19 pandemic had no substantial impact on the assessment of the study objectives.
L602: Protocol deviations were managed according to the protocol deviation guidance provided by the Sponsor and were reviewed by the Sponsor.
L603: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L604: FDA agrees with the Applicant's assessment of important protocol deviations. The dataset
L605: included 213 important violations (166 occurring on the tebentafusp arm, 45 occurring in the IC arm, and 2 involving use of incorrect versions of the informed consent document (ICD) occurring in patients not enrolled). Important protocol violations were categorized as violations in:
L606: - Discontinuation criteria, including treatment past progression without signing specific treatment past progression informed consent form, or treatment past confirmed progression;
- Inclusion/ Exclusion criteria, including laboratory assessments not meeting criteria, missing ECG records, no measurable disease, randomization prior to baseline testing, failure to screen and inform patients of childbearing potential of contraception requirements.
- Informed Consent/ HIPAA, including use of wrong ICD or failure to re-consent with updated ICF, screening/ screening labs prior to signing ICD,
- Investigational Product, including patient refusal to stay overnight in hospital as required by protocol, product administered beyond 4 hours (all &lt; 10 minutes), missed doses due to freezer or other technical problem with product, incorrect storage or freezer failure, administration of incorrect dose (2 events involving incorrect dosing of 68 mg instead of 20 mg on day 1 cycle 1).
- Prohibited medication, specifically administration of pre-medication which was not allowed per protocol prior to administration of C1D1 for 1 patient.
- Delayed SAE reporting.
- Trial procedures including missed laboratory assessments, scan completed outside of window, missed post dose vital signs, incorrect timing of laboratory assessments.
L607: There were more Trial Procedure violations in the tebentafusp arm than occurred in the IC arm. This can be explained by the increased frequency of treatment, increased complexity of administration and monitoring, and lack of familiarity with the investigational product. The violations do not necessitate removal of any patients from the analysis. The protocol deviations described above are unlikely to have had a meaningful impact on interpretation of the study results.
L608: ## Table of Demographic Characteristics
L609: ## **Data:**
L610: Overall, the majority of patients were White (87.0%) and approximately half were male (50.3%), with a median age of 64.0 years (range, 23 to 92 years) (Table 18). Approximately half of the patients were \ge 65 years of age. Nearly two-thirds of patients were from the non-North American region (63.5%). By country, most patients were enrolled in the US (31.7%) and Germany (15.6%). The vast majority of patients (94.5%) had an ECOG performance status score of 0 or 1 at baseline. One patient, in the investigator's choice arm, had an ECOG performance status score of 2, which is an eligibility criteria deviation and was accounted for in the important protocol deviations.

L619: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L620: FDA agrees with the Applicant's summary of the patients' demographic characteristics and agrees that they were generally balanced across the treatment groups. The median age of patients in the study was 64, slightly older than what has been cited in the literature as (59 to 62 years) (Kaliki 2017). The study included very few patients of American Indian/Alaskan Native race and Hispanic or Latino ethnicity. No patients were reported to be Black/African American or Asian, however this appears to be consistent with observed incidences of uveal melanoma in different racial and ethnic groups.

L639: ## **Treatment Compliance, Concomitant Medications, and Rescue Medication Use**
L640: ## **Data:**
L641: Data are presented below as part of the applicant's position.
L642: ## **The Applicant's Position:**
L643: ## **Treatment compliance**
L644: The median relative dose intensity was similar in both treatment arms: 100.0% (range, 95% to 100%) in the tebentafusp arm and 100% (range, 100% to 100%) in the investigator's choice arm.
L645: ## **Concomitant medications**
L646: Concomitant medications were defined as medications that (1) started before the first dose of study drug and were continuing at the time of the first dose of study drug, or (2) started on or after the date of the first dose of study drug (or, started at the time of or after the first dose of study drug) up to 90 days following the last dose.
L647: A total of 245 patients (97.2%) in the tebentafusp arm and 108 patients (85.7%) in the investigator's choice arm received at least 1 concomitant medication. The most common classes of concomitant medications received (by &gt; 50% of patients in either the tebentafusp arm or investigator's choice arm) were analgesics (89.7% vs 41.3%), antihistamines for systemic use (86.1% vs 18.3%), blood substitutes and perfusion solutions (56.7% vs 14.3%), and corticosteroids, dermatological preparations (54.0% vs 14.3%). The drug classes of analgesics, antihistamines, blood substitutes, anti-inflammatory products, and corticosteroids were received by a notably higher percentage of patients in the tebentafusp arm because they were used as preventive or therapeutic interventions for management of rash and CRS, which are AEs associated with the mechanism of action of tebentafusp.
L648: ## **Subsequent anticancer therapies**
L649: After discontinuation of study drug, 109 patients (43.3%) in the tebentafusp arm and 55 patients (43.7%) in the investigator's choice arm received other systemic anticancer therapies. The median duration of subsequent anticancer treatment was 110.0 and 70.0 days, respectively.
L650: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L651: FDA agrees with the Applicant's assessments.
L652: ## Efficacy Results – Primary Endpoint (Including Sensitivity Analyses)
L653: Data:
L654: ## **Overall survival – ITT Analysis Set**
L655: In the ITT Analysis Set, based on a median follow-up duration of 14.1 months and 150 death events (87 [34.5%] in the tebentafusp arm and 63 [50.0%] in the investigator's choice arm), the primary endpoint of OS favored tebentafusp with a HR of 0.51 (95% CI: 0.37, 0.71; p &lt; 0.0001; Table 20 and Figure 27). Although not yet mature, the Kaplan-Meier estimates of median OS was longer in the tebentafusp arm (21.7 months; 95% CI: 18.6, 28.6) compared with the investigator's choice arm (16.0 months; 95% CI: 9.7, 18.4). Kaplan-Meier estimates of OS at 12 and 24 months were 73.2% and 44.8%, respectively, for tebentafusp, and 58.5% and 20.3%, respectively, for investigator's choice (Table 20).

L666: ## **Overall survival sensitivity analysis**
L667: Overall survival results were consistent across all prespecified sensitivity analyses.
L668: ## **The Applicant's Position:**
L669: Tebentafusp demonstrated a significant improvement in OS, compared with investigator's choice, in HLA-A*02:01-positive patients with mUM who had not received prior systemic therapy in the metastatic setting. Tebentafusp reduced the relative risk of death by 49%, compared with investigator's choice (HR 0.51; 95% CI: 0.37, 0.71; p &lt; 0.0001). Based on a median follow-up duration of 14.1 months, treatment with tebentafusp resulted in a 6-month improvement in estimates of median OS compared with investigator's choice (21.7 vs 16.0 months), and an approximate 15% increase in 1-year OS (73.2% vs 58.5%). Overall survival results were consistent across all prespecified sensitivity analyses.
L670: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L671: ## **Primary analysis of OS:**
L672: FDA generally agrees with Applicant's summary of the primary analysis results of OS in the all-randomized population. FDA notes that with 150 OS events observed at this interim analysis (60% of the total 250 events required for final OS analysis), the corresponding stopping boundary was 0.006. FDA also notes that, while all the landmark/milestone rates presented for OS were validated by the FDA reviewers, these were considered as supportive information only.
L673: ## ***Sensitivity analysis of OS:***
L674: FDA acknowledges and agrees with the Applicant's position that OS results were consistent across all prespecified sensitivity analyses. FDA notes that, as per SAP, the only planned sensitivity analysis of OS included considering a stratified analysis based on strata determined by the central LDH results if there are &gt;10% discrepancies observed in LDH results when compared to the strata recorded at the time of randomization in the IRT system, which was the prespecified source of stratification that to be used in the primary analysis of OS. The Applicant noted that fewer than 10% of patients had inconsistencies between their randomized LDH stratum and their appropriate LDH stratum per the central lab data and therefore no additional sensitivity analysis were performed based on this criteria. In addition to this prespecified sensitivity analysis, the Applicant also reported the OS results in the RAS analysis set and safety analysis set.
L675: ## ***Subgroup Analyses of OS:***
L676: Forest plots for OS by subgroups for Study 202 are presented in Figure 39, Section 8.1.6. FDA notes that all subgroup analyses are considered exploratory. In subgroups with adequate sample sizes, the OS results were consistent with the overall OS results. For further discussion on OS subgroup analysis refer to FDA's Assessment on Page-156 of this Assessment Aid.

L736: ## The Applicant's Position:
L737: Tebentafusp demonstrated a statistically significant improvement in the secondary endpoint of PFS, compared with investigator's choice. Tebentafusp reduced the relative risk of disease progression or death by 27%, compared with investigator's choice (HR 0.73; 95% CI: 0.58, 0.94; p = 0.0139). Disease control rate at 12 weeks was numerically higher with tebentafusp than investigator's choice (45.6% vs 27.0%). The point estimate for ORR was higher with tebentafusp than investigator's choice (9.1% vs 4.8%). Best overall response will be formally tested in a later analysis after all patients have undergone at least 3 planned on-treatment tumor assessments.
L738: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L739: 111
Version date: January 2020 (ALL NDA/ BLA reviews)
L740: *Disclaimer: In this document, the sections labeled as "Data" and "The Applicant's Position" are completed by the Applicant and do not necessarily reflect the positions of the FDA.*
L741: While FDA generally agrees with the Applicant's summary of the secondary endpoint results, FDA has the following assessments regarding these results:
L742: - FDA does not agree with the Applicant's conclusion that tebentafusp demonstrated a clinically significant improvement in PFS over IC in Study 202. Although the HR of 0.73 (95% CI: 0.58, 0.94) for PFS favored the tebentafusp arm, the improvement in median PFS was only 0.4 months, and while the p-value of 0.0139 would be considered statistically significant, FDA does not consider an improvement of less than 2 weeks to be clinically meaningful. The tumor responses were assessed every 12 weeks. In addition, the 95% CI for the PFS probability at all landmark timepoints for the tebentafusp and IC arms overlap. The lack of benefit with respect to PFS can also be noted from the KM plot of PFS curves, where separation between the curves over the entire follow-up period was very marginal.
- The tumor response rates observed in Study 202 are marginal and effectively the same for both arms. The 95% CI's overall with the lower bound of the 95% CI is under 6% for both arms.
L743: FDA also has the following additional comments :
L744: - As per the SAP, PFS was planned to be tested only once after observing at least 275 events and therefore, the observed p-value of 0.0139 for PFS analysis was compared to an alpha of 0.05 to demonstrate statistical significance.
- FDA acknowledges that ORR was not formally tested at this interim analysis.
- FDA does not object to the Applicant's inclusion of DCR in their summary in this review; however, this endpoint was not considered as part of the overall benefit:risk assessment(b) (4).
- All the landmark/milestone rates presented for PFS and DOR were validated by the FDA reviewers but these were considered as supportive information only.

L748: ## The Applicant's Position:
L749: Justification for the Phase 3 tebentafusp dose and regimen is based on clinical data driven by safety, tolerability, and clinical activity from the dose escalation phases of Study 01 and Study 102 with supporting data from the expansion phase of Study 102 rather than tebentafusp exposure-response. Validation of the tebentafusp regimen is provided by the Phase 3 results, including high OS (hazard ratio [HR] 0.51 for OS; Table 20) and overall tolerability (low rate of tebentafusp-related discontinuations [2.4% Phase 2, 2.0% Phase 3] and no tebentafusp-related
L750: deaths.

L760: ## **Persistence of Effect**
L761: ## **Data:**
L762: Data are presented below are part of the applicant's position.
L763: ## **The Applicant's Position:**
L764: The Phase 3 Study 202 is an ongoing open-label, randomized, multi-center study to evaluate the efficacy and safety of tebentafusp versus investigator's choice (pembrolizumab, ipilimumab, or dacarbazine) in adult HLA-A*02:01-positive patients with mUM who had not received prior systemic therapy in the metastatic setting. All patients randomized to tebentafusp received the intra-patient escalation regimen of 20 mcg on Day 1, 30 mcg on Day 8, 68 mcg on Day 15, and 68 mcg once every week thereafter. Tebentafusp was administered via intravenous infusion over 15 to 20 minutes. Treatment continued until radiographic progression, unacceptable toxicity, investigator decision, or patient withdrawal of consent. Patients who received tebentafusp, pembrolizumab, or ipilimumab and experienced disease progression per RECIST could continue with treatment beyond initial RECIST progression until confirmed RECIST progression, if they met the prespecified criteria.
L765: In the ITT Analysis Set, based on a median follow-up of 14.1 months, the median duration of treatment was 163.0 days (range, 1 to 1016 days) with tebentafusp and 65.0 days (range, 1 to 658 days) with investigator's choice. A total of 172 patients (68.3%) in the tebentafusp arm and 100 patients (79.4%) in the investigator's choice arm had discontinued treatment, while 73 patients (29.0%) continued to receive tebentafusp and 11 patients (8.7%) continued to receive investigator's choice. The most common reason for permanent discontinuation of study drug was
L766: disease progression, which occurred in 154 patients (61.1%) in the tebentafusp arm and 78 patients (61.9%) in the investigator's choice arm.
L767: In Phase 3 Study 202, only patients receiving tebentafusp and, in the investigator's choice arm, only patients receiving pembrolizumab were treated beyond initial RECIST progression and included in the treatment beyond progression analyses. No crossover to tebentafusp was permitted; investigators were free to choose subsequent therapy. In the ITT Analysis Set, the incidence of treatment beyond initial RECIST progression was higher in patients randomized to the tebentafusp arm compared with those randomized to the investigator's choice arm (43.3% [109/252] vs 14.3% [18/126]).
L768: To better understand the role of treatment beyond progression, a post-hoc exploratory analysis was conducted in the subset of patients who had a BOR of PD. Among patients with a BOR of PD, the incidence of treatment beyond initial RECIST progression was higher with tebentafusp than pembrolizumab (53.4% [70/131] vs 17.5% [11/63]). In these patients, the median duration of treatment beyond progression was longer with tebentafusp than pembrolizumab (1.7 vs 0.8 months).
L769: To eliminate immortal time bias and to capture the majority of PD patients after the first tumor assessment at Day 84 (\pm 7 days), a landmark OS analysis was conducted that was limited to patients with a BOR of PD alive at Day 100. Patients with a BOR of PD are traditionally considered to not be benefitting from therapy and by definition, do not have indolent SD, and so OS could not be confounded by slow growing tumor.
L770: In this landmark OS analysis, 105 of 252 patients (41.7%) in the tebentafusp arm versus 53 of 126 patients (42.1%) in the investigator's choice arm were alive and had a best response of PD by Day 100. In these patients, OS was superior for tebentafusp compared with investigator's choice (HR 0.43; 95% CI: 0.27, 0.68; Figure 31), even when considering key baseline covariates. These data provide a potential explanation for why tebentafusp can have a low RECIST-based response rate but dramatic OS benefit and suggest that radiographic-defined PD for tebentafusp may not always equate with lack of benefit or a reason for treatment discontinuation.
L771: **Figure 31** Post-landmark Overall Survival in Patients with Best Overall Response of Progressive Disease in Study 202
L772: ## NDA/BLA Multi-disciplinary Review and Evaluation BLA 761228
## Kimmtrak (tebentafusp)
L773: CI = confidence interval; HR = hazard ratio; IMCgp100 = tebentafusp.
L774: Source: Module 2.7.3, Figure 5.
L775: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L776: Post hoc analyses, including those of the responder-analysis of treatment past progression, were not considered as part of FDA's overall risk:benefit assessment of tebentafusp.
L777: In general, FDA does not agree that the argument presented above supports the Applicant's conclusion. A comparison of patients in the tebentafusp arm who were treated past progression with patients in the pembrolizumab arm who were treated past progression does not support a conclusion that any observed benefit was due to treatment past progression. Any improvement in OS observed in patients in the tebentafusp arm who were treated past progression when compared to patients in the pembrolizumab arm could have been derived before being treated past progression. To assess whether treatment past progression provides benefit to the patients, the Applicant must demonstrate that patients in the tebentafusp arm who were treated past progression had superior outcome to patients in the tebentafusp arm who were not treated past progression by randomly assigning the patients to these two groups.
L778: ## **Efficacy Results – Secondary or exploratory COA (PRO) endpoints**
L779: ## **Data:**
L780: ## **Health-related quality of life**
L781: Health-related quality-of-life data were collected using the EORTC QLQ-C30 and EQ-5D,5L PRO instruments.
L782: ## ***EORTC-QLQ-C30***
L783: In both the tebentafusp and investigator's choice arms, patients were considered to be domain compliant (ie, completed at least 50% of the EORTC QLQ-C30 items) through C17D1, with generally similar rates between the arms. Subsequently, patients in the tebentafusp arm remained domain compliant through C29D1, whereas compliance in the investigator's choice arm decreased to approximately 33% at C29D1.
L784: At baseline, no differences in EORTC-QLQ-C30 scores were observed between the treatment arms for any of the domains. In general, throughout the study, the EORTC-QLC-C30 scores were similar between the treatment arms and remained stable for most domains. However, statistically significant and clinically meaningful LS mean improvements from baseline were observed for fatigue at EOT (10.9 vs 20.1; p = 0.0445) and insomnia at C5D1 (-9.3 vs 2.8; p = 0.0176), both favoring tebentafusp, and for constipation at EOT (3.2 vs -3.5; p = 0.0296), favoring investigator's choice. LS mean scores over time are illustrated in Figure 32 for PRO symptoms of fatigue, insomnia, and constipation.

L790: Overall, there was no significant difference between the tebentafusp and investigator's choice arms for time to sustained deterioration across the different EORTC-QLQ-C30 domains.
L791: ## *EQ-5D, 5L*
L792: In both the tebentafusp and investigator's choice arms, patients were considered to be domain compliant through C17D1, with generally similar rates between the arms. Subsequently, patients in the tebentafusp arm remained domain compliant through C29D1, whereas compliance in the investigator's choice arm decreased to 40.0% at C21D1 and 33.3% at each of C25D1 and EOT.
L793: At baseline, no differences in EQ-5D, 5L scores were observed between the treatment arms for any of the domains. In general, throughout the study, mean change from baseline was similar between the treatment arms for all domains.
L794: ## The Applicant's Position:
L795: Health-related quality of life, as assessed by the EORTC-QLQ-C30 and EQ-5D,5L PRO instruments, was generally similar between the 2 treatment groups, with some symptoms showing improvement in the tebentafusp group.
L796: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L797: PROs were included as secondary endpoints in Study 202; however, no PRO endpoints were included in the formal testing plan, so all PRO analyses are considered to be exploratory and no claims can be made based on these analyses. Thus, FDA does not agree with the Applicant's conclusion of "statistically significant and clinically meaningful LS mean improvements from baseline" for specific PRO domains such as fatigue, insomnia, and constipation. FDA also does not agree with the Applicant's conclusion of "no significant difference between the tebentafusp and investigator's choice arms for time to sustained deterioration across the different EORTC QLQ-C30 domains" and the Applicant's statement that health related quality of life was generally similar between the two treatment groups. Such claims in particular cannot be made as the trial was not powered to show non-inferiority and was not designed with pre-specified non-inferiority margins for the domains.
L798: FDA has the following additional comments regarding the PRO analyses for this trial:
L799: - The EORTC-QLQ-C30 and EQ-5D, 5L instruments are completed at baseline, at C1D1, at Day 1 of every other cycle through C5D1, then every 4th cycle thereafter beginning with C9D1 until (and including at) end of treatment (during the treatment period).
- FDA agrees with the Applicant's summary of the completion rates and the domain compliant rates as presented.
- While EORTC-QLQ-C30 maybe a reasonable choice to assess some functional impacts, the Global Health Status/QoL metric does not necessarily reflect a meaningful assessment of core disease, treatment symptoms, and their associated impact.
- The EQ-5D-5L is a composite that incorporates self-reported ability to function, pain, and general health status as filled out by the patient. This instrument is a generic preference based measure intended to provide a health utility index value for use in economic analyses and lacks content validity for use in estimating clinical benefit for the
L800: purposes of labeling claims, though FDA acknowledges that this instrument is often used by other regulatory authorities and/or payers.
L801: ## Additional Analyses Conducted on the Individual Trial
L802: ## Data:
L803: Data are presented below as part of the applicant's position. These data were also presented above to support persistence of effects.
L804: ## The Applicant's Position:
L805: There is accumulating evidence in the field of immuno-oncology, initially described for ipilimumab, that some patients treated with checkpoint inhibitors may develop immune-related response patterns beyond traditional RECIST response. While the most widely cited example of this is tumor regression despite the appearance of new lesions, other examples include a slow, steady decline in tumor burden and initial PD of the tumor prior to subsequent disease stabilization (Wolchok, 2009). The practical implication is that survival benefit may still occur despite initial disease progression, and thus, progression should be confirmed prior to discontinuation of immunotherapy (Hodi, 2016).
L806: Examples of the immune-related response patterns to tebentafusp were noted in Phase 1, with predominantly a slow, steady decline in tumor burden and some patients who had initial PD followed by disease stabilization (Middleton, 2015; Middleton, 2016). In Study 102 Phase 2 expansion, a low RECIST response rate but promising OS coupled with examples again of these types of immune responses suggested that immune-related response may predominate for tebentafusp. Intriguingly, approximately 42% of patients (25/60) with a best RECIST response of PD, including some with an increase in tumor burden as best change on treatment, had survival longer than 1 year.
L807: In clinical practice, standard of care for checkpoint inhibitors includes the option for treatment beyond initial progression because of the concept of immune-related responses. However, no Phase 3 study with checkpoint inhibitors has demonstrated that immune-related responses are sufficiently high to account for an OS benefit.
L808: In Phase 3 Study 202, only patients receiving tebentafusp and, in the investigator's choice arm, only patients receiving pembrolizumab were treated beyond initial RECIST progression and included in the treatment beyond progression analyses. No crossover to tebentafusp was permitted; investigators were free to choose subsequent therapy. In the ITT Analysis Set, the incidence of treatment beyond initial RECIST progression was higher in patients randomized to the tebentafusp arm compared with those randomized to the investigator's choice arm (43.3% [109/252] vs 14.3% [18/126]).
L809: To better understand the role of treatment beyond progression, a post-hoc exploratory analysis was conducted in the subset of patients who had a BOR of PD. Among patients with a BOR of
L810: PD, the incidence of treatment beyond initial RECIST progression was higher with tebentafusp than pembrolizumab (53.4% [70/131] vs 17.5% [11/63]). In these patients, the median duration of treatment beyond progression was longer with tebentafusp than pembrolizumab (1.7 vs 0.8 months).
L811: To eliminate immortal time bias and to capture the majority of PD patients after the first tumor assessment at Day 84 (\pm 7 days), a landmark OS analysis was conducted that was limited to patients with a BOR of PD alive at Day 100. Patients with a BOR of PD are traditionally considered to not be benefitting from therapy and by definition, do not have indolent SD, and so OS could not be confounded by slow growing tumor.
L812: In this landmark OS analysis, 105 of 252 patients (41.7%) in the tebentafusp arm versus 53 of 126 patients (42.1%) in the investigator's choice arm were alive and had a best response of PD by Day 100. In these patients, OS was superior for tebentafusp compared with investigator's choice (HR 0.43; 95% CI: 0.27, 0.68; Figure 31), even when considering key baseline covariates. These data provide a potential explanation for why tebentafusp can have a low RECIST-based response rate but dramatic OS benefit and suggest that radiographic-defined PD for tebentafusp may not always equate with lack of benefit or a reason for treatment.
L813: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L814: FDA does not consider post hoc analyses and does not agree with the Applicant that any conclusions can be made regarding efficacy in the subgroup of patients that were treated beyond progression. The lack of randomization is unable to account for potential differences in inherent patient characteristics that may influence the interpretation of this post hoc analysis. See FDA comment above.
L815: ## 8.1.3. Supportive Study 102
L816: ## Trial Design
L817: ## The Applicant's Description:
L818: ## **Overall design**
L819: This was a Phase 1/2 study of tebentafusp administered on a QW basis with an intra-patient escalation dosing regimen (Figure 33). The intra-patient escalation regimen consisted of fixed low doses of tebentafusp at C1D1 (20 mcg) and C1D8 (30 mcg), followed by an escalated cohort specified QW dose administered at C1D15 and beyond. According to this regimen, all patients in the study received 2 QW doses of tebentafusp at a dose level below the identified RP2D-QW (50 mcg; determined in Study IMCgp100-01), and then dose escalation commenced at the third QW dose at C1D15 with the goal to achieve a long-term dosing regimen at a dose higher than that identified for the straight QW dosing regimen (RP2D-QW). Doses between 54 and 73 mcg were evaluated in the Phase 1 dose escalation cohorts. Dose escalation identified the RP2D-IE; 68 mcg was selected for Phase 2 dose expansion.
L820: **Figure 33** Study 102 Design
L821: PHASE I	PHASE II

L822: IV = intravenous(ly).
L823: The Phase 1 portion of the study had a standard 3 + 3 dose escalation design. After the dose escalation portion was complete and the MTD or the RP2D-IE was identified, two Phase 2 dose expansion cohorts in mUM were to be enrolled. The Phase 2 dose expansion Cohort A was to enroll patients with mUM in the second-line setting after disease progression following systemic treatment with a checkpoint inhibitor and to recruit approximately 20 patients. Phase 2 dose expansion Cohort B was to enroll patients with mUM in the second- or third-line settings with up to 1 prior line of LDT and to recruit approximately 130 patients. However, in practice, there was significant overlap between the Cohort A and Cohort B populations, and it was not clear that patients belonged distinctly in either cohort. Therefore, this report will present data for the single combined Phase 2 dose expansion cohort. This expansion cohort was designed to further characterize the safety, tolerability, and preliminary PK and antitumor activity of tebentafusp.
L824: ## **Eligibility criteria**
L825: Key inclusion criteria included:
L826: - ≥ 18 years of age at the time of informed consent.
- Histologically or cytologically confirmed mUM.
- Cohort A: Disease progression on 1 systemic treatment regimen containing a checkpoint inhibitor. Any prior LDT was acceptable.1
- Cohort B: Disease progression on 1 or 2 prior lines of therapy, including chemotherapy, immunotherapy, or targeted therapy. Prior LDT was counted as a line of therapy.1
- HLA-A*02:01 positive by central assay.
- ECOG performance status score of 0 or 1.
L827: Key exclusion criteria included:
L828: - Systemic or untreated CNS metastases.
L829: 1 In practice, there was significant overlap between Cohort A and Cohort B populations, and it was not clear that patients belonged distinctly in either cohort. Therefore, the data were presented for a single combined Phase 2 expansion cohort.
L830: - History of severe hypersensitivity reactions to other iologic drugs or mAbs.
- Clinically significant cardiac or impaired cardiac function.
- Active infection that required systemic antibiotic therapy.
- Known HIV.
- Active hepatitis B virus or hepatitis C virus infection.
- Adrenal insufficiency.
- Treatment with systemic steroid therapy or any other immunosuppressive medication at any dose level.
- Radiotherapy within 2 weeks of first dose of study drug.
- Use of hematopoietic colony-stimulating growth factors.
- Systemic anticancer therapy within 2 weeks of the first dose of study drug.
- Requiring chronic, systemic corticosteroid therapy at any dose for longer than 2 weeks.
L831: ## **Dose selection**
L832: The selection of intra-patient dose escalation over the first 3 weeks was identified in FIH, Phase 1 Study 01. Several key observations in Study 01 led to recommending an intra-patient escalation regimen using fixed doses. First, in Study 01, patients with higher body weights who received higher absolute doses of tebentafusp experienced more severe treatment-related toxicity. Consequently, fixed doses, rather than weight-based doses, were incorporated during the study. Second, given no clinically meaningful differences in safety or efficacy were identified between the weekly and daily dosing regimens, and in light of the easier utility of a weekly regimen, subsequent evaluation focused on weekly dosing. Third, the emerging safety profile of tebentafusp suggested that CRS (and associated hypotension) was more likely to occur during the first 2 weeks, after which tachyphylaxis occurred. Therefore, as has been used for other T cell-engaging bispecifics, an intra-patient escalation regimen during Weeks 1 and 2 (ie, 20 and 30 mcg, respectively) prior to dosing 50 mcg was selected as the preferred regimen to evaluate further in Study 102.

L843: ## Statistical Analysis Plan and Amendments
L844: ## The Applicant's Description:
L845: ## **Analysis populations**
L846: Four analysis sets were used in this study, as defined in Table 24.
L847: **Table 24: Study 102: Analysis Populations**
L848: Analysis Set	Definition
All Patients Enrolled Set	The All Patients Enrolled Set comprised all patients who provided the main informed consent for this study. Patients who signed prescreening informed consent but not the main informed consent did not appear in this analysis set. The All Patients Enrolled Set was used to summarize patient disposition and the analysis sets. Any patients who signed the main informed consent, but never started the study drug for any reason were regarded as screen failures. For these patients, the eCRF data collected was not to be included in summary analyses but was to be reported in the clinical study report as part of the listings for patient disposition, treatment allocation and eligibility criteria.
Full Analysis Set	The FAS comprised all patients assigned to study drug and who received at least 1 full or partial dose of tebentafusp. The FAS was used for all demography, baseline characteristics, and efficacy data summaries and analyses.
Safety Analysis Set	The Safety Analysis Set included all patients who received at least 1 full or partial dose of tebentafusp. Patients were classified in this set according to initial treatment received. The Safety Analysis Set was used for the safety summary of the study.
Per-protocol Set	The PPS consisted of a subset of FAS patients in the Phase 2 dose expansion cohort who met the following criteria:Presence of measurable disease at baseline according to Investigator assessment based on RECIST v1.1.
One or more postbaseline tumor assessment or discontinue prior to the first tumor assessment.
Received at least 1 full or partial dose of treatment.
No violation of key inclusion or exclusion criteria as described in the SAP.
Patients in the PPS were classified according to planned treatment. The PPS defined the patients used in the sensitivity analysis of the primary efficacy endpoint (ie, ORR) and 1 of the secondary endpoints (DOR) for the Phase 2 dose expansion cohort only.
L849: DOR = duration of response; FAS = Full Analysis Set; ORR = objective response rate; PPS = Per-protocol Set; RECIST = Response Evaluation Criteria in Solid Tumors; SAP = statistical analysis plan.

L867: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L868: FDA acknowledges and agrees with the Applicant's summary of key elements and the trial design for Study 102.
L869: The Applicant met with FDA on October 12, 2018, to discuss the proposed development plan and registrational strategy for tebentafusp. During this meeting, the Applicant proposed an accelerated approval pathway supported by ORR data (with a target lower 95% confidence limit of \ge 5\% for confirmed ORR) submitted from Study 102 and supportive data from Study 202 available at the time of the planned interim analysis of Study 102.
L870: FDA expressed uncertainty whether an ORR endpoint from a single-arm trial would be able to accurately and reliably predict clinical benefit in the intended patient population and whether the proposed target effect, that would exclude a lower 95% confidence limit of \ge 5\% for
L871: confirmed ORR, would demonstrate a clinically meaningful improvement in anti-tumor response or long-term disease benefit. At the time of this meeting tebentafusp had been observed to have a significant toxicity profile in the setting of a modest response rate further limiting the ability to conclude this product had a favorable risk:benefit profile based on ORR from a single-arm trial.

L886: ## **Patient Disposition**
L887: ## **Data:**
L888: Patient disposition is summarized in Figure 34 for the Phase 1 dose escalation and Phase 2 dose expansion cohorts. The DCO date for this study was 20 March 2020.
L889: **Phase 1 dose escalation cohorts:** Of the 21 patients screened, 19 patients were enrolled into 4 cohorts: 3 in Cohort 1 54 mcg, 6 in Cohort 2 64 mcg, 4 in Cohort 3 73 mcg, and 6 in Cohort 4 68 mcg. All 19 (100%) enrolled patients received study drug, of whom 18 (94.7%) patients discontinued study drug and 1 (5.3%) patient remained on treatment. At the DCO date, the duration of exposure for the patient who remains on treatment was 42.1 months. The primary reason for treatment discontinuation was disease progression (89.5%). At the data cut-off date, a total of 5 (26.3%) patients remained on study and 14 (73.7%) patients were reported to have ended the study (all due to death).
L890: **Phase 2 dose expansion cohort:** Of the 148 patients screened, 127 patients were enrolled into the Phase 2 dose expansion part of the study. All 127 (100%) patients received study drug, of whom 106 (83.5%) patients discontinued study drug and 21 (16.5%) patients remained on treatment. The primary reason for treatment discontinuation was disease progression (70.1%). At the data cut-off date, 53 (41.7%) patients remained on study and 74 (58.3%) patients were reported to have ended the study. Death was the primary cause of study discontinuation (54.3%).

L911: ## **Table of Demographic Characteristics**
L912: ## Data:
L913: In the Phase 2 dose expansion cohort, the majority of patients were White (99.3%) and approximately half were female (50.7%), with a mean age of 60.4 years (range, 25 to 88 years) (Table 28). There was a higher proportion of patients who were &lt;65 years of age (63.0%) versus &gt;65 years of age (37.0%). The majority of patients (70.1%) had an ECOG performance status score of 0 at baseline. A total of 58.3% of patients had baseline LDH &gt;ULN, 80.3% of patients had baseline ALC \ge 1.0 \times 10^9/L, and 70.9% of patients had baseline ALP \leULN.

L924: ## **Other Baseline Characteristics (e.g., disease characteristics, important concomitant drugs)**
L925: ## Data:
L926: ## **Baseline disease characteristics**
L927: For most patients in the Phase 2 expansion cohort, the choroid was the initial UM site (75.7%) and metastasis was not observed at initial diagnosis (92.3%) (Table 29). Approximately one-third of patients had LDH &gt; ULN 250 U/L (36.0%), a poor prognostic factor for advanced UM, and more than half of the patients' largest metastatic lesion recorded at baseline was ≤ 3 cm (55.3%). For the majority of patients (79.9%), the pre-randomization choice of therapy was pembrolizumab. Most patients did not receive prior surgery for metastatic disease (91.3%).

L939: ## **Prior medications**
L940: All Phase 2 dose expansion patients received at least 1 prior anticancer therapy in the metastatic setting, two-thirds of patients received 1 line of prior anticancer therapy, and one-third of patients received at least 2 lines of prior anticancer therapy. The most commonly reported prior anticancer therapies were systemic therapies (83.5%, of which immunotherapy was reported in 73.2% of patients), radiotherapy (75.6%), LDT (44.9%), and surgery (40.9%). Forty-three (33.9%) patients received \ge 2 lines of prior anticancer therapy. The most commonly reported prior anticancer therapy regimen was radiotherapy (77.2%).
L941: ## The Applicant's Position:
L942: The disease characteristics and prior therapies of the mUM population enrolled in Study 102 were generally consistent with the target mUM population of pretreated patients with poor prognostic indicators.

L980: ## Efficacy Results – Secondary and other relevant endpoints
L981: ## Data:
L982: ## **Disease control rate**
L983: Of the 127 patients in the Phase 2 expansion cohort, 57 (44.9%) patients had SD at ≥8 weeks per RECIST v1.1 according to ICR (
L984: Table 30). The DCR was 31.5% (40 patients; 95% CI: 23.5%, 40.3%) at ≥16 weeks and 22.8% (29 patients; 95% CI: 15.9%, 31.1%) at ≥24 weeks.
L985: ## **Duration of Response**
L986: Among patients with an objective response in the Phase 2 expansion cohort, the median duration of response was 8.7 months (95% CI: 5.6, 24.5) (Table 31). The estimated percentage of ongoing responders was 60.0% (95% CI: 12.6%, 88.2%) at 6 months and 20.0% (95% CI: 0.8%, 58.2%) at 12 months. Results were similar for the FAS and PPS.

L994: ## **Time to response**
L995: Among patients with an objective response in the Phase 2 expansion cohort, the mean (Std) time to response was 7.0 months (6.9), with response times ranging from 1.6 to 20.5 months.
L996: ## **Rate and duration of minor response**
L997: In the Phase 2 expansion cohort, the rate of MinR or better (CR, PR, or MinR) was 11.0% (14 of 127 patients; 95% CI: 6.2%, 17.8%). The median duration of MinR or better was 10.3 months (95% CI: 5.8, 23.2). The estimated percentage of responders (including those with a MinR) was 78.6% (95% CI: 47.2%, 92.5%) at 6 months and 33.3% (95% CI: 10.9%, 58.0%) at 12 months.

L999: Tumor size was presented graphically using a waterfall plot, to present each patient's best percent change from baseline in tumor size and BOR as assessed by RECIST v1.1 by ICR for the Phase 2 dose expansion cohort in Figure 35. Overall, the waterfall plots illustrate benefit beyond traditional PR defined by RECIST v1.1.

L1016: ## **Progression-free survival**
L1017: In the Phase 2 expansion cohort, the median PFS was 2.8 months (95% CI: 2.0%, 3.7%) (Figure 37). Of the 127 patients in the Phase 2 dose expansion cohort, the estimated PFS rates were 25.0% (95% CI: 17.8%, 32.9%) at 6 months and 10.9% (95% CI: 6.2%, 17.2%) at 12 months. A total of 117 (92.1%) patients had the event of disease progression (106 [83.5%] patients) or death in the absence of disease progression (11 [8.7%] patients).
L1018: **Figure 37** Study 102: Kaplan-Meier Plot of Progression-free Survival (RECIST v1.1 by Independent Central Review) - Phase 2 Dose Expansion
L1019: CI = confidence interval; ICR = Independent Central Review
L1020: Events were either disease progression or death in the absence of disease progression, which occurred within 2 tumor assessment visits of the last evaluable assessment. Events that did not occur within 2 tumor assessment visits were censored. Tumor assessment was based on RECIST v1.1 by ICR.
L1021: Source: Module 5.3.5.2, Study 102 CSR, Figure 14.2.2.3.
L1022: ## **Overall survival**
L1023: In the Phase 2 expansion cohort, the median duration of follow-up was 19.6 months (95% CI: 16.0, 22.2). The median OS was 16.8 months (95% CI: 12.9, 21.3) (Figure 38). Sixty-nine patients died and 58 patients were censored for any reason. Of the 127 patients in the Phase 2 dose expansion cohort, the OS rates were 61.8% (95% CI: 52.6%, 69.8%) at 12 months and 37.0% (95% CI: 26.5%, 47.5%) at 24 months.
L1024: **Figure 38** Study 102: Kaplan-Meier Plot of Overall Survival - Phase 2 Dose Expansion
L1025: CI = confidence interval.
L1026: Events were deaths due to any cause. Patients not known to have died at the time of analysis are censored.
L1027: Source: Module 5.3.5.2, Study 102 CSR, Figure 14.2.3.2.
L1028: ## The Applicant's Position:
L1029: Together, these findings indicate that responses were durable and patients derived benefit beyond the traditional PR defined by RECIST.
L1030: Tebentafusp demonstrated encouraging survival benefit. After a median follow-up of 19.6 months, the median OS was 16.8 months. Although this was a single-arm study, when compared with historical data (median OS &lt; 12 months; Khoja, 2019), the OS results are promising in mUM patients, many of whom were heavily pretreated and had poor prognostic factors.

L1046: ## **Efficacy Results – Secondary or exploratory COA (PRO) endpoints**
L1047: ## **Data:**
L1048: ## **Health-related quality of life**
L1049: Health-related quality-of-life data were collected using the EORTC QLQ-C30 and EQ-5D,5L PRO instruments.
L1050: ## ***EORTC-QLQ-C30***
L1051: There was no deterioration (based on a threshold of 10 points) at Cycle 1, Cycle 5, or Cycle 9 across all dimensions: physical functioning, role functioning, cognitive functioning, and social functioning, nausea, vomiting, pain, dyspnea, insomnia, appetite loss, constipation, diarrhea, financial difficulties, and global health status/quality of life.
L1052: ## ***EQ-5D, 5L***
L1053: The EQ-5D-5L questionnaire compliance results were 75.3% at baseline (expected, 93 according to protocol version 7 or later, of which 70 evaluable), 78.7% at C3D1, 80.4% at C5D1, and 82.1% on C9D1.
L1054: Mean EQ-5D index and EQ-VAS scores at baseline were 0.8109 and 77.2, respectively, and not significantly different to that of the general population norms (0.779 for index and 77.8 for EQ-VAS). There was no deterioration in either EQ-5D or EQ-VAS at Cycle 1, Cycle 5, or Cycle 9, or at the EOT (based on MID= 0.075 or 7.5, respectively) (Szende, 2014).
L1055: ## **The Applicant's Position:**
L1056: Health-related quality of life, as assessed by the EORTC-QLQ-C30 and EQ-5D,5L PRO instruments, showed no deterioration in patients treated with tebentafusp.
L1057: ## **Additional Analyses Conducted on the Individual Trial**
L1058: ## **Data:**
L1059: Not applicable
L1060: ## **The Applicant's Position:**
L1061: Not applicable
L1062: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L1063: FDA has reviewed the Applicant's description of the conduct of Study 102, including compliance with GCP, patient characteristics and disposition, treatment compliance and concomitant medications, data quality and integrity along with the Applicant's analyses of primary, secondary, and exploratory endpoints; however, as this is a supportive study, FDA did not verify the analyses and results are considered exploratory.
L1064: FDA considers the analysis of PFS and OS in Study 102 to be descriptive in nature, as results of
L1065: these endpoints in a single-arm study are uninterpretable. Similarly, FDA notes that no conclusions should be drawn from PRO analyses in a single arm study.
L1066: The observed response rate was 5% in Study 102, creating significant uncertainties with respect to risk-benefit based on the single arm trial. Accordingly, randomized Study 202 was necessary to assess whether tebentafusp provided benefit to patients, especially given the potential for life-threatening cytokine release syndrome.
L1067: ## 8.1.5. Integrated Review of Effectiveness
L1068: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L1069: Study 202 is the primary study supporting the efficacy claim for this BLA. Study 102 was submitted as a supportive study. Study 202 and Study 102 were evaluated separately, and no integrated review of effectiveness was performed.
L1070: ## 8.1.6. Assessment of Efficacy Across Trials
L1071: When comparing the efficacy results between Phase 3 Study 202 and the Study 102 Phase 2 expansion, key differences between the 2 studies, should be considered. Briefly, these include the following: (1) Study 202 enrolled patients who had not received prior systemic therapy in the metastatic setting (1L), whereas Study 102 Phase 2 expansion enrolled patients who were previously treated in the metastatic setting (2L+); (2) Study 202 included a control group (investigator's choice) whereas Study 102 was a single-arm study; and (3) the primary endpoint was OS in Study 202 and ORR in Study 102 Phase 2 expansion (ORR and OS were secondary endpoints in Study 202 and Study 102, respectively).
L1072: ## Primary Endpoints
L1073: ## **Data:**
L1074: ## **Overall survival**
L1075: Table 32 summarizes OS in Phase 3 Study 202 and Study 102 Phase 2 expansion.
L1076: In Study 202, based on a median follow-up duration of 14.1 months and 150 death events in the ITT Analysis Set, the primary endpoint of OS significantly favored tebentafusp with a HR of 0.51 (95% CI: 0.37, 0.71; p &lt; 0.0001) (Table 20 and Figure 27 above). Although not yet mature, the Kaplan-Meier estimates suggest median OS was significantly prolonged in the tebentafusp arm compared with the investigator's choice arm (21.7 vs 16.0 months). Kaplan-Meier estimates of OS at 12 and 24 months were 73.2% and 44.8%, respectively, for tebentafusp, and 58.5% and 20.3%, respectively, for investigator's choice.
L1077: In Study 102 Phase 2 expansion, based on a median follow-up duration of 19.6 months and 69 death events in the FAS, the median OS was 16.8 months (Figure 38 above). Kaplan-Meier
L1078: estimates of OS at 12 and 24 months were 61.8% and 37.0%, respectively.

L1083: ## The Applicant's Position:
L1084: Overall, tebentafusp demonstrated a statistically significant and clinically meaningful OS benefit compared with investigator's choice in HLA-A*02:01-positive patients with mUM who had not received prior systemic therapy in the metastatic setting in Study 202, confirming the promising OS observed in Study 102 Phase 2 expansion in HLA-A*02:01-positive patients with previously treated mUM.
L1085: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L1086: FDA's assessment of efficacy is based primarily on Study 202. FDA considered Study 102 separately only as a supportive trial. Comparisons between the trials were not performed due to the limitations of cross trial comparisons. In addition, event endpoints like OS and PFS are uninterpretable in single arm trials.
L1087: ## Secondary and Other Endpoints
L1088: ## **Data:**
L1089: ## **Progression-free survival**
L1090: Table 33 summarizes PFS in Phase 3 Study 202 and Study 102 Phase 2 expansion.
L1091: In Study 202, based on a median follow-up duration of 11.4 months and 295 PD or death events, PFS favored tebentafusp and met statistical significance (HR 0.73; 95% CI: 0.58, 0.94; p = 0.0139) (Table 21 and Figure 28 above). Median PFS was 3.3 months in the tebentafusp arm and 2.9 months in the investigator's choice arm, and 6-month PFS rates were 30.9% and 18.9%, respectively.
L1092: In Study 102 Phase 2 expansion, based on a median follow-up duration of 25.8 months and 117 PD or death events, the median PFS was 2.8 months and 6-month PFS rate was 25.0% (Figure 37).

L1097: ## **Tumor response**
L1098: Table 34 summarizes tumor response in Phase 3 Study 202 and Study 102 Phase 2 expansion.
L1099: In Study 202, ORR (investigator-reported) was numerically higher with tebentafusp than investigator's choice (9.1% vs 4.8%) (Table 34). In the tebentafusp arm, BORs were 1 CR (0.4%), 22 PRs (8.7%), 92 SDs (36.5%), and 131 PDs (52.0%). In the investigator's choice arm, there were no CRs; 6 patients (4.8%) had PRs, 28 (22.2%) had SD, and 78 (61.9%) had PD. Disease control rate (defined as CR + PR + SD \ge 12 weeks) favored tebentafusp compared with
L1100: investigator's choice, with rates of 45.6% and 27.0%, respectively. The median DOR was similar between the 2 treatment arms (9.9 vs 9.7 months).
L1101: In Study 102 Phase 2 expansion, the ORR (IRC-reported) was 4.7% (Table 34). There were no CRs; 6 patients (4.7%) had PRs, 57 (44.9%) had SD, and 60 (47.2%) had PD. Disease control rate (defined as CR + PR + SD \ge 24 weeks) was 22.8%. The median DOR was 8.7 months.

L1108: ## The Applicant's Position:
L1109: Tebentafusp demonstrated favorable PFS in both Study 202 and Study 102 Phase 2 expansion and rather with a statistically significant benefit over investigator's choice in patients with mUM who had not received prior systemic therapy in the metastatic setting in Study 202.
L1110: Tumor response rates were modest in both Study 202 and Study 102 Phase 2 expansion. However, the response rate was numerically higher for tebentafusp compared with investigator's choice in patients with mUM who had not received prior systemic therapy in the metastatic setting in Study 202. Across both studies, RECIST based endpoints consistently underestimated the OS benefit from tebentafusp.
L1111: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L1112: Comparisons between the trials were not performed due to the limitations of cross trial comparisons. In addition, event endpoints like OS and PFS are uninterpretable in single arm trials.
L1113: FDA agrees that ORR in Study 202 was not consistent with the observed OS and does not appear to be an appropriate endpoint to predict the OS benefit seen in the tebentafusp arm.

L1132: ## The Applicant's Position:
L1133: Overall, the OS benefit demonstrated with tebentafusp in Study 202 was generally observed across all prespecified major demographic and known prognostic subgroups. In Study 102 Phase 2 expansion, the trends in median OS and 1-year OS for each subgroup was generally similar to those observed in the tebentafusp arm of Study 202.
L1134: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L1135: While FDA does not object to the Applicant's presentation of the results of the OS analysis in Studies 202 and 102, FDA cautions against cross-study comparisons due to limitations inherent in such comparisons and the potential impact of differences between the studies.
L1136: FDA agrees with the Applicant's position that the OS benefit seen in Study 202 is consistent across the subgroups analyzed. The greatest difference in HR for OS is observed in subgroup analysis based on LDH, which was a stratification factor for Study 202. In Study 202, the HR for OS is 0.35 (95% CI: 0.21, 0.60) in patients with LDH \le 250 U/L and 0.70 (95% CI: 0.46, 1.09) in patients with LDH &gt; 250 U/L. However, FDA considers all subgroup analyses as exploratory only.
L1137: ## Additional Efficacy Considerations
L1138: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L1139: None
L1140: ## 8.1.7. Integrated Assessment of Effectiveness
L1141: ## Data:
L1142: Data are presented below as part of the applicant's position.
L1143: ## The Applicant's Position:
L1144: The efficacy of tebentafusp is clearly established in mUM. In the pivotal Phase 3 Study 202 in HLA-A*02:01-positive patients with mUM who had not received prior systemic therapy in the metastatic setting, tebentafusp demonstrated a significant improvement in OS, reducing the relative risk of death by 49% compared with investigator's choice of either pembrolizumab, ipilimumab, or dacarbazine. The estimated 1-year OS rate was 73.2% for tebentafusp and 58.5% for investigator's choice. The superior tebentafusp OS benefit was generally observed across all subgroups analyzed, including patients with poor prognostic factors. These data confirm the promising OS observed in the supportive Study 102 Phase 2 expansion in HLA-A*02:01-positive patients with previously treated mUM.
L1145: Tebentafusp significantly improved PFS, reducing the relative risk of disease progression or death by 27%, compared with investigator's choice. Moreover, both DCR at 12 weeks (45.6% vs 27.0%) and the point estimate for ORR (9.1% vs 4.8%) were higher with tebentafusp than investigator's choice. As observed for other immunotherapies, RECIST response rate underestimated the OS benefit from tebentafusp.
L1146: While Study 202 was not designed to identify the contribution of treatment beyond initial progression, investigators were permitted by the protocol to allow for treatment beyond initial progression for tebentafusp and pembrolizumab. At the time of the DCO, just under half (43.3%) of patients in the tebentafusp arm were treated beyond initial progression; 21.5% of the total tebentafusp treatment administered during the study was delivered after the initial tumor progression. Based on a landmark OS analysis from Study 202 in patients with BOR of PD, tebentafusp provides a dramatic OS benefit, resulting in a 57% reduction in the relative risk of death over investigator's choice. These data suggest that a best response of PD is not a sign of lack of benefit and should not be the sole reason for discontinuation of tebentafusp treatment.
L1147: Together, tebentafusp showed a highly significant OS benefit versus investigator's choice in the Phase 3 Study 202 in HLA-A*02:01-positive patients with mUM who had not received prior systemic therapy in the metastatic setting. The control arm was generally consistent with historical reported trials confirming that tebentafusp provides substantial improvement over available therapies (Rantala, 2019; Pelster, 2021; Piulats, 2021). Tebentafusp is the first systemic treatment to demonstrate a statistically significant and clinically meaningful survival benefit in mUM, representing an essential treatment option for patients with this serious and life-threatening disease.
L1148: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L1149: The analysis of effectiveness of tebentafusp was based on data submitted from 378 patients enrolled to Study 202. FDA agrees that tebentafusp demonstrated a statistically significant and clinically meaningful improvement in OS over investigator choice for patients with HLA-A*02:01-positive metastatic uveal melanoma who had not received prior systemic therapy in the metastatic setting (HR of 0.51 [95% CI: 0.37, 0.71], p &lt; 0.0001). The results of Study 202 support full approval for this indication.
L1150: FDA does not agree with the Applicant's analysis of treatment past progression, and no conclusions regarding benefit of continuing treatment with tebentafusp after evidence of progressive disease can be drawn.

L1153: The clinical development program of tebentafusp in melanoma is comprised of 5 Immunocore sponsored clinical studies, including 2 completed studies (Phase 1, IMCgp100-01 [Study 01];

Across these clinical studies, 587 patients with melanoma, including 410 patients with uveal melanoma (UM), have been exposed to tebentafusp monotherapy.
