L1163: ## 8.2.1. Safety Review Approach
L1164: ## The Applicant's Position:
L1165: Two pools of safety data will be considered: Pool 1 comprising UM patients (Uveal Melanoma Patients) and Pool 2 comprising all melanoma patients who received tebentafusp as monotherapy (All Melanoma Monotherapy Patients).
L1166: - **Pool 1 (Uveal Melanoma Patients):** Safety data will be reported side by side for UM patients from Study 01 (including rollover Study 401 data), Study 102 dose-escalation patients (Phase 1), Study 102 dose-expansion patients (Phase 2), Study 202 (tebentafusp arm only), pooled data for the tebentafusp arms of Study 102 (dose escalation and expansion) and Study 202, and pooled data for all studies. The uveal melanoma pool (n=410) provides primary evidence of safety and tolerability of tebentafusp monotherapy in the target metastatic uveal melanoma patient population regardless of line of therapy, where the randomized Study 202 (n=245) provides the majority of the safety data.
- **Pool 2 (All Melanoma Monotherapy Patients):** Safety data will be reported side by side for Study 01 (including rollover Study 401 data), Study 102 (combining dose escalation and expansion), Study 202 (tebentafusp arm only), monotherapy patients from Study 201, and pooled data for all studies. A supportive assessment of safety and tolerability of tebentafusp monotherapy is provided in the all melanoma pool (N=505). This safety dataset allows characterization of the safety profile of tebentafusp as a monotherapy, in patients across tumor types and regardless of line of therapy. The pool will be used to assess any difference in profile due to adding of cutaneous melanoma patients. Table 37 shows the number of patients from each study included in Pools 1 and 2.

L1169: a20/30/68 mcg refers to the dosing regimen applied throughout the study. The proposed dosing regimen is 20 mcg at Cycle 1 Day 1, 30 mcg at Cycle 1 Day 8, and 68 mcg at Cycle 1 Day 15 and weekly thereafter.
L1170: When comparing the two pools of safety data, there is consistency of results between UM and all melanoma pool. Therefore, the primary presentation in this document will be done using the UM pool of patients.
L1171: Overall, tebentafusp has a well-established safety profile across the 5 Immunocore-sponsored clinical studies in 587 patients with melanoma, including 410 patients with UM, who were exposed to tebentafusp monotherapy. The randomized Study 202 provides the primary safety data and is proposed to be the basis for labeling presentation of Warning and Precautions and Adverse Events sections of the USPI. While the Study 102 safety data are consistent with Study 202 and supportive of the proposed indication and dosing regimen.
L1172: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L1173: The primary data used by FDA to assess and characterize the safety of tebentafusp at the proposed recommended dosage (20 mcg on Day 1, 30 mcg on Day 8, 68 mcg on Day 15, and 68 mcg once every week thereafter until disease progression, unacceptable toxicity, or death) is derived from the pivotal trial, Study 202, in which 245 patients received at least 1 dose of tebentafusp and 111 patients received at least 1 dose of investigator's choice agent.
L1174: Patients enrolled to Study 202 were required to be 18 years old or older. Key exclusion criteria for this study impacting interpretation of safety data include:
L1175: - Serum creatinine &gt; 1.5 \times ULN and/or creatinine clearance (calculating using Cockcroft-Gault formula, or measured) &lt; 50 mL/minute
- Total bilirubin &gt; 1.5 \times ULN, except for patients with Gilbert's syndrome who are excluded if total bilirubin &gt; 3.0 \times ULN or direct bilirubin &gt; 1.5 \times ULN
- Alanine aminotransferase &gt; 3 \times ULN, Aspartate aminotransferase &gt; 3 \times ULN
- Absolute neutrophil count &lt; 1.0 \times 10^9/L, Absolute lymphocyte count &lt; 0.5 \times 10^9/L
- Platelet count &lt; 75 \times 10^9/L, Hemoglobin &lt; 8 g/dL
- Clinically significant cardiac disease or impaired cardiac function (NYHA Grade \ge 2, QTcF &gt; 470 msec, acute myocardial infarction or unstable angina pectoris &lt; 6 months prior to screening.
- Known history of HIV infection
- Active HBV or HCV infection
- History of adrenal insufficiency, interstitial lung disease, pneumonitis that required corticosteroid treatment or current pneumonitis, history of colitis of inflammatory bowel disease
L1176: The protocol included dose modifications for tebentafusp for the following toxicities:
L1177: - Skin Toxicity Pruritis:For Grade 3 events, hold tebentafusp until \le Grade 1
- Rash/PhotosensitivityFor Grade 2 and 3 events, hold tebentafusp until \le Grade 1
- For Grade 4 events, permanently discontinue tebentafusp
- HypotensionFor Grade 4 events, dose reduce or permanently discontinue tebentafusp
L1178: - Infusion-related Reactions/Anaphylaxis (occurring during infusion)For Grade 3 events that do not improve by at least 1 grade within 6 hours with medical management or Grade 4 toxicity, permanently discontinue tebentafusp
- Infusion-related Reactions (occurring after infusion/Cytokine Release Syndrome (based on Lee 2014 grading criteria))For Grade 3 events, hold tebentafusp until resolution of symptoms and restart after discussion and written approval of Sponsor's Medical Monitor
- For Grade 4 events, permanently discontinue tebentafusp
- Hepatic Function AbnormalitiesFor Grade 3 or 4 events, hold tebentafusp until \le Grade 1
- VomitingFor Grade 3 or 4 events, hold tebentafusp until \le Grade 1
- Other Adverse Events:For Grade \ge 3 events, hold study drug until Grade 1.
L1179: The protocol-specified dose reductions of tebentafusp for toxicity is as follows: from a starting tebentafusp dose of 68 mcg, the dose will be reduced to 54 mcg for any toxicity requiring dose reduction. The dose may be reduced further to 50 mcg for recurrent toxicity. Patients who require more than 2 dose reductions of tebentafusp should discontinue treatment.
L1180: To confirm the Applicant's safety signals identified in Study 202 and to identify any additional, rare, but potentially serious safety signals, FDA reviewed the Applicant's analyses for Pool 1 and separately analyzed Applicant Pool 2 which comprised 505 patients with uveal or cutaneous melanoma who received at least 1 dose of tebentafusp at any dose level. For FDA's analyses, Applicant Pool 2 is referred to as ISS_Pool. The analysis of the ISS_Pool is summarized in Section 8.2.11. In its safety analysis, FDA did not consider safety analyses of individual studies other than the pivotal trial Study 202.
L1181: FDA calculated the frequency of all treatment emergency adverse events (TEAEs) at the PT, HLT, HLGT, SOC MedDRA levels as well as using standard narrow, broad, and algorithmic SMQs, and FDA Office of Oncologic Disease (OOD) defined grouped terms (GT) for Study 202 and the ISS_Pool. The FDA defined group terms are a set of related preferred term and are defined in Appendix 19.3. Use of a grouped term is denoted by (GT) following the group name e.g., Fatigue (GT) comprises the preferred terms fatigue and asthenia. In its analysis of TEAEs, FDA considered all reported events regardless of investigator assessment of relatedness.
L1182: Analysis of the adverse reaction of cytokine release syndrome (CRS) is discussed in detail along with the other adverse events of special interest (AESIs), which include skin reaction/rash and hepatotoxicity, in Section 8.2.5.
L1183: For safety assessments based on laboratory and vital signs, FDA considered the objective data
L1184: rather than adverse event reporting. In general, FDA has not included TEAE reporting for laboratory investigations in TEAE frequency tables (i.e., in labeling).
L1185: ## 8.2.2. Review of the Safety Database

L1220: ## The Applicant's Position:
L1221: Demographics and baseline characteristics were generally similar across studies with some differences in baseline characteristics (i.e., elevated lactate dehydrogenase [LDH], largest liver lesion diameter, time from initial diagnosis to study treatment) related to first line (1L) vs. second line or greater (2L+) population.
L1222: Overall, approximately half of the patients were male (51%), with a median age of 62 years (range 23 to 91 years). More than half of the patients were between 18 and &lt; 65 years (57.3%) and 42.7% of patients were ≥ 65 years of age. The majority of patients (75.4%) had an ECOG performance status score of 0 (fully active). Approximately one-third of patients had an initial diagnosis of stage II disease and nearly one-quarter of patients had a stage III diagnosis. The vast majority of patients (95.4%) had a liver lesion, 48.5% of which were &lt; 3 cm and 46.8% of which were ≥ 3 cm.
L1223: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L1224: Table 41 summarizes the key demographic characteristics for the safety analysis set of Study 202. Overall, the two arms were well balanced with respect to gender, age, ECOG performance status, race, and ethnicity. FDA notes that although uveal melanoma occurs much less frequently in Black/African American, Asian, and American Indian/Alaskan Native patients, these populations appeared to be underrepresented in Study 202.

L1230: ## Adequacy of the safety database:
L1231: ## The Applicant's Position:
L1232: Based on the incidence of metastatic uveal melanoma in the US and globally, a safety database of 410 uveal melanoma patients treated with tebentafusp is representative and considered adequate to determine the overall safety profile of tebentafusp. The integrated safety findings are consistent with the safety observed in the randomized Study IMCgp100-202, which is the study proposed as the primary basis for labeling of safety in the USPI.
L1233: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L1234: The size of the safety database is adequate to provide a reasonable estimate of adverse reactions that may be observed with tebentafusp at the proposed dosage; duration of the treatment is adequate to allow assessment of adverse reactions over time. The safety database represents the gender, age, and race consistent with that observed in the overall population of patients who are diagnosed with uveal melanoma. However, the lack of data for patients who identify as Black/African American, Asian and American Indian/Alaskan Native may limit the generalizability of conclusions to these populations.
L1235: ## 8.2.3. Adequacy of Applicant's Clinical Safety Assessments
L1236: ## Issues Regarding Data Integrity and Submission Quality
L1237: ## The Applicant's Position:
L1238: The rationale for integrating safety data was to provide an assessment of the overall safety profile of tebentafusp and to increase precision around the likely incidence of rare events. However, differences in study design, including eligibility criteria and dosing regimens, should be considered when interpreting the pooled safety data. Given the key design differences among the studies, integrated safety tables will present the individual study results as well as the pooled results. No tables will present pooled results only.
L1239: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L1240: FDA performed the following analyses to assess the quality and consistency of the safety data:
L1241: - Comparison of coding: The FDA reviewer compared the coding of the actual reported term (AETERM) to the lowest level term in the MedDRA hierarchy (AELLT) for the 9448 treatment emergent adverse events (TEAEs) that were reported for patients enrolled on Study 202. Overall the coding was consistent and appropriate. The reporting of Cytokine Release Syndrome by investigators likely underrepresents the incidence. This is discussed further in Section 8.2.5. This does not represent an issue with data integrity or a coding error.
- Comparison of treatment discontinuations due to adverse events as reported in the subject level ADSL, which includes patient disposition, and as reported in ADAE variable AECN (action taken) did not identify significant discrepancies.
- Comparison of dose delays and modifications due to adverse events as reported in the exposure data file and as reported in adverse events did not identify significant discrepancies.
- Comparison of assessment of relatedness to study treatment for fatalities and serious adverse events based on evaluation of narratives did not identify significant discrepancies.
- FDA confirmed the safety results reported by the Applicant in this review and provided in the label.
L1242: ## Categorization of Adverse Event
L1243: ## Data:
L1244: Categorization of AEs are described below.
L1245: ## The Applicant's Position:
L1246: For the integrated analyses, AEs were coded using the Medical Dictionary for Regulatory
L1247: Activities (MedDRA) Version 23.1 or later and graded based on the National Cancer Institute Common Terminology Criteria for Adverse Events (NCI CTCAE) Version 4.03. Data from studies originally reported in previous versions of MedDRA were recoded to the most current version for the integrated summary of safety (ISS) database.
L1248: For pooling of TEAE data, the incidence of each preferred term (PT) and system organ class (SOC) was pooled across the relevant studies based on the original individual study reporting and derivations using the Analysis Data Model datasets. A TEAE was defined as any AE that occurred on or after the date of initial receipt of study drug. The analysis of AEs (as describe in this SCS) was limited to TEAEs. Any AEs considered to be non-treatment emergent are presented in the individual CSRs only. All TEAEs were summarized descriptively by MedDRA SOC and PT count with (n) and percentage (%).
L1249: Based on data from ongoing and completed tebentafusp clinical studies, the following adverse events of special interest (AESI) have been specified: CRS, acute skin reactions, and LFT elevations. Other events of interest include cardiac and eye disorders were identified in Studies 202 and 102. These events are discussed further in Section 8.2.5.
L1250: **The FDA's Assessment:**
FDA agrees.
L1251: ## Routine Clinical Tests
L1252: ## The Applicant's Position:
L1253: The SeCore® HLA Sequencing System and uTYPE® Dx v1.0 HLA Sequence Analysis Software developed by Thermo Fisher Scientific have been used as a companion diagnostic (CDx) to identify HLA-A*02:01 positive patients for tebentafusp clinical studies.
L1254: **The FDA's Assessment:**
In Study 202, hematology and chemistry laboratory assessment values were graded for severity according to NCI-CTCAE 4.03. The schedule for vital sign and laboratory assessments depended on the treatment to be administered. For patients randomized to receive tebentafusp, vital signs were performed before and after dosing according to protocol and institutional standards (minimum every 4 hours for 16 hours C1D1, C1D8, C1D15). Laboratory assessments (hematology and chemistry panels) were assessed weekly prior to each dose. Patients were also evaluated for safety events with physical examination and adverse event monitoring.

L1268: ## The Applicant's Position:
L1269: Across the tebentafusp clinical development program, the vast majority of deaths were due to disease progression. None of the deaths were considered to be related to tebentafusp by the investigator.
L1270: Across all studies, a total 173 patients (42.2%) died. The most common cause of death was disease progression in 165 patients (40.2%); followed by deaths due to “other” reasons in 4 patients (1%); two patients (0.5%) who died due to “unknown” reasons; and two patients (0.5%) who died due to adverse events. None of these were considered related to tebentafusp.
L1271: Among the 4 deaths where the primary cause was due to “other” reasons, the free text descriptions in the CRF included the following: “fell downstairs and had stroke” 163 days after tebentafusp was discontinued due to disease progression [Study 102, Patient [REDACTED] (b) (6)], “clinical disease progression” [Study 102, Patient [REDACTED] (b) (6)] and “unknown” for two patients in Study 202 [Patient [REDACTED] (b) (6) and Patient [REDACTED] (b) (6)]. Patient [REDACTED] (b) (6) died 235 days after tebentafusp was discontinued due to disease progression, while the death for Patient [REDACTED] (b) (6) occurred 27 days after the last tebentafusp dose and was due to metastatic uveal melanoma and unrelated to tebentafusp per investigator.
L1272: The two deaths where the primary cause was due to “unknown” reasons occurred in Study 01 [Patient [REDACTED] (b) (6) and Patient [REDACTED] (b) (6)] 143 and 173 days, respectively, after treatment with tebentafusp was discontinued due to disease progression.
L1273: The two deaths where the primary cause was due to AEs occurred on Study 202 (pneumonia [Patient [REDACTED] (b) (6)], pulmonary embolism [Patient [REDACTED] (b) (6)]). None of these two deaths were considered related to study drug by the investigator.
L1274: All deaths in Studies 01, 102 and 202 were reviewed to ensure no casualty with tebentafusp; narratives were written on all of these patients and included in the relevant CSRs.
L1275: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L1276: Table 43 summarizes deaths occurring in patients enrolled to Study 202 based on reporting in the ADSL. Of patients enrolled to the tebentafusp arm, 84 patients (34%) had died as of data cutoff. As noted by the Applicant, almost all patients died due to disease progression. In the tebentafusp arm, 2 patients (0.8%) died as a result of adverse event, only one of these occurred within 30 days of the last dose of tebentafusp.
L1277: Review of TEAE reporting (ADAE) identifies one Grade 5 event of pulmonary embolism (0.4%) in the tebentafusp arm and 2 Grade 5 events of pulmonary embolism and left ventricular dysfunction (1.8%) in the IC arm occurring within 30 days of the last dose of study drug.

L1286: For the ISS_Pool dataset, 0.8% of patients (4/505) had fatal adverse events. These were intra-abdominal hemorrhage, malignant melanoma, pneumonia, and pulmonary embolism. The event of abdominal hemorrhage occurred in a 44 year old gentleman with a diagnosis of metastatic cutaneous melanoma. The patient received his first dose of tebentafusp at a dose of 0.0006 mg/kg. Six days later, the patient experienced fatal abdominal hemorrhage after presenting to the emergency department with right upper quadrant pain, dyspnea, hypoxia, and no palpable pulse. The patient died despite aggressive resuscitation. The cause of death was stated as abdominal hemorrhage, possibly related to liver biopsy performed before study treatment was given and repeated 2 days later. Both the Investigator and Sponsor assessed the
L1287: event as unrelated to administration of tebentafusp but possibly related to the study procedure. There is insufficient information to assess causality.
L1288: *Reviewer Comment:* Fatal adverse events within 90 days of the last dose of tebentafusp are rare. Across the development program in 505 patients who received at least one dose of tebentafusp monotherapy, there were no fatal adverse events that are assessed by this reviewer, based on the limited information contained in the narratives, as related to tebentafusp.

L1300: Adverse events (AEs) are coded using MedDRA version 23.1.
L1301: TEAEs are defined as any AE with a start date from day of first dose of study drug up to 90 days after last dose of study drug or until start of alternative cancer therapy post treatment discontinuation, whichever occurs first.

L1305: ## The Applicant's Position:
L1306: Across all studies, 128 patients (31.2%) experienced an SAE. The most common SAEs reported were CRS in 29 patients (7.1%), hypotension in 11 patients (2.7%), and pyrexia in 16 patients (3.9%).
L1307: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L1308: Table 46 shows the frequency of SAEs occurring in Study 202. Overall, the incidence of SAEs was slightly higher in the tebentafusp arm compared with the IC arm. The pattern of SAEs between the two arms differs. The most common SAEs reported in the tebentafusp arm are CRS, events associated with CRS including nausea, dyspnea, and hypotension, and skin disorders including rash, pruritis, urticaria, and skin reaction. These SAEs were rarely reported in patients receiving IC.

L1317: In the ISS_Pool, 36% of patients (182/505) had at least one SAE. The most commonly reported event by PT occurring in \ge 2\% of patients were CRS (6%), pyrexia (3.4%), and hypotension (2.8%). An additional SAE of note is multiple organ dysfunction syndrome (Grade 4) occurring in 2 patients (0.4%).
L1318: Multiple organ dysfunction syndrome occurred in 1 patient enrolled to the dose expansion phase of Study 102. Patient (b) (6) was a 53 year old woman with a diagnosis of uveal melanoma who developed Grade 3 CRS and Grade 4 hypotension after her first dose of tebentafusp. The patient was treated with fluid resuscitation, steroids, phenylephrine, vasopressor, and tocilizumab. The day after the infusion, the patient developed Grade 4 multiple organ dysfunction syndrome including acute kidney injury, respiratory failure, and shock liver. The patient was intubated. The patient was ultimately extubated and transitioned to hemodialysis. The event of CRS was reported as resolved; the event of multiple organ dysfunction was ongoing at the time the patient was discharged to hospice. The patient subsequently died of progressive disease 27 days after the patient's only dose of study drug. The SAE of multiple organ dysfunction syndrome was ongoing at the time of death.
L1319: Multiple organ dysfunction syndrome occurred in 1 patient, a 78 year old gentleman, enrolled to study 201. Narrative was not available.
L1320: **Reviewer Comment:** Across the development program, the most frequently observed SAEs are consistent with those observed in Study 202 and associated with CRS. The potentially fatal
L1321: event of multiple organ system dysfunction, which may be associated with CRS, was reported in 0.4% of patients.
L1322: ## Dropouts and/or Discontinuations Due to Adverse Effects
L1323: ## Data:
L1324: Table 47 presents drug-related TEAEs resulting in study drug discontinuation and is presented by SOC and PT for UM patients for the individual studies and all studies combined. The choice to present a summary of study drug discontinuation due to drug-related TEAEs per Investigator, rather than TEAE regardless of causality, is because this is how the investigators made the decision to continue or discontinue study drug during the study. In general, drug-related TEAEs are more likely to exclude TEAE resulting from disease progression or underlying morbidity.

L1367: ## **The Applicant's Position:**
L1368: Across all studies, 107 patients (26.1%) experienced TEAE that led to study drug discontinuation. The most common TEAEs that led to study discontinuation regardless of causality were pyrexia in 10 patients (2.9%), ALT increased in 10 patients (2.4%), and AST increased in 10 patients (2.4%).
L1369: Across all studies, TEAEs that led to study drug interruption and considered to be drug-related by the Investigator were reported in 72 patients (17.6%). This approach enables identification of AEs the Investigator deemed drug-related vs. interruptions that are more related to disease progression or other comorbidities.
L1370: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L1371: FDA did not consider investigator assigned causality in its safety analysis.
L1372: In Study 202, the percentage of patients who had at least one TEAE leading to treatment interruption is similar between the arms with 62 patients (25%) in the tebentafusp arm and 27 patients (24%) in the IC arm having at least one AE leading to interruption. Table 50 summarizes the TEAEs leading to treatment interruption in at least 2 patients in the tebentafusp arm.
L1373: In the ISS_Pool, 24% of patients (121/505) had at least one TEAE leading to treatment interruption. This is similar that observed in Study 202. The most common TEAE leading to discontinuation were consistent with those identified in Study 202, including CRS and AEs associated with CRS.

L1392: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L1393: SAEs are discussed earlier in Section 8.2.4.
L1394: ## Grade 3-4 TEAES
L1395: FDA considered Grade 3-4 TEAES as significant adverse events. Table 52 shows Grade 3-4 TEAES, not including investigator AE reported laboratory abnormalities, occurring in at least 2% of patients in the tebentafusp arm in Study 202. A higher percentage of patients in the tebentafusp arm (54%) had at least one Grade 3-4 TEAE compared to patients in the IC arm (34%). As seen in the analysis of SAEs, most Grade 3-4 TEAES in the tebentafusp arm were CSR, related to CSR, or related to skin toxicity. Overall, the Grade 3-4 analysis did not identify any new safety signal with the exception of hypertension. In patients who received tebentafusp, 9% had the adverse event of hypertension. As noted by the Applicant in Section 8.2.5, because of the risk of hypotension associated with tebentafusp related CRS, Studies 102 and 202 required patients with pre-existing hypertension to temporarily hold anti-hypertensive medications prior to the first few doses of tebentafusp. This may explain the higher frequency of hypertension seen in the tebentafusp arm compared to the IC arm.

L1433: ## The Applicant's Position:
L1434: For all studies combined, a total of 410 patients (100.0%) who received tebentafusp experienced any TEAE. System organ classes in which TEAEs were reported most frequently (\ge 50\% of patients in tebentafusp arm) were general disorders and administration site conditions (95.6%), skin and subcutaneous tissue disorders (94.6%), gastrointestinal disorders (83.7%), vascular disorders (57.8%), musculoskeletal and connective tissue disorders (57.3%), nervous system disorders (55.4%), and investigations (53.2%).
L1435: TEAEs (PTs) with the highest incidence (\ge 30\% of patients in tebentafusp arm) were pyrexia (78.5%), pruritus (72.0%), fatigue (56.8%), nausea (57.1%), chills (54.9%), rash (51.0%), hypotension (41.5%), vomiting (36.1%), dry skin (35.1%), headache (32.9%), rash maculopapular (32.7%), and oedema peripheral (32.0%).
L1436: These TEAEs are consistent with the mechanism of action of tebentafusp and can generally be divided into 2 major categories, skin and cytokine-mediated events. Tebentafusp redirects T cells to gp100+ melanocytes in the skin which leads to acute skin toxicities (rash, pruritus, erythema, and edema). Tebentafusp also activates T cells leading to cytokine release which results in cytokine mediated AEs including CRS (defined by pyrexia, hypotension, and hypoxia) as well as other AEs likely resulting from elevated cytokines (e.g., fatigue, nausea, chills, vomiting and headache). Other less frequent TEAEs included LFT, ocular events, and cardiac events.
L1437: Of the 410 patients who received tebentafusp, 408 (99.5%) reported TEAEs that were considered related to study drug by the investigator. A total of 195 patients (47.6%) had treatment-related TEAEs that were of Grade 3 or 4 severity. Serious TEAEs were reported in 128 patients (31.2%) and 90 (22.0%) reported serious TEAEs were considered related to study drug. Discontinuation of study drug due to TEAEs was uncommon and occurred in 15 patients (3.7%) with 8 (2.0%) patients experiencing events that were considered related to study drug. TEAEs resulting in death were reported in 1 patient (0.2%) in Study 202 (Patient [REDACTED](b)(6); pulmonary embolism) and was considered not related to study drug by the investigator.
L1438: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L1439: In the ISS_Pool, 100% of the 505 patients had at least 1 TEAE. Analysis of the TEAEs at all levels of the MedDRA hierarchy and using SMQ analysis did not identify any new or more severe safety signal beyond those identified in Study 202. No rare but potentially severe or life-threatening adverse reactions associated with tebentafusp was identified that was not already observed in Study 202.
L1440: **Table 55** shows the most frequent TEAEs, occurring in \ge 15\% of patients in the tebentafusp arm, for Study 202. While the percentage of patients experiencing at least 1 TEAE of any grade are similar between the tebentafusp and the IC arms, more patients in the tebentafusp arm had \ge 1 Grade 3-4 TEAE compared to patients in the IC arm.
L1441: In the ISS_Pool, 100% of the 505 patients had at least 1 TEAE. Analysis of the TEAEs at all levels of the MedDRA hierarchy and using SMQ analysis did not identify any new or more severe safety signal beyond those identified in Study 202. No rare but potentially severe or life-threatening adverse reactions associated with tebentafusp was identified that was not already observed in Study 202.

L1453: ## **The Applicant's Position:**
L1454: Clinically relevant laboratory abnormalities include lymphocyte count decreased, phosphate decreased, lipase increased, amylase increased, ALT/AST increased, and bilirubin increased. Other blood chemistry changes that occurred in ≥ 20% patients receiving tebentafusp included hemoglobin decreased, creatinine increased, glucose increased, albumin decreased, calcium decreased, alkaline phosphatase increased, magnesium decreased, and sodium decreased. These laboratory findings were not considered clinically relevant and very few to none were ≥Grade 3. Clinically relevant liver function tests for labelling included AST increase, ALT increase, and bilirubin increase.
L1455: Overall, the clinical chemistry and hematology changes observed during Study 202 were generally mild to moderate in severity and required no treatment or dose modification.
L1456: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L1457: Table 57 summarizes the incidence of laboratory abnormalities worsening from baseline for Study 202. The most common worsening laboratory abnormalities, occurring in ≥50% of patients on the tebentafusp arm, were lymphocyte count decreased (91%), creatinine increased (87%), glucose increased (66%), AST increased (54%), ALT increased (52%), phosphate decreased (51%), and hemoglobin decreased (51%). Laboratory assessments worsening to Grade 3 in ≥ 5% of patients in the tebentafusp arm occurred only for lymphocyte count decreased (41%), lipase increased (13%), AST increased (12%), phosphate decreased (12%), and ALT increased (8%). Laboratory assessments worsening to Grade 4 in ≥ 5% of patients in the tebentafusp arm occurred only for lymphocyte count decreased (15%).
L1458: While laboratory abnormalities were common in patients who received tebentafusp, most were Grade 1 or 2. Many of the laboratory abnormalities were those associated with CRS.

L1464: ## Vital Signs
L1465: ## The Applicant's Position:
L1466: A decrease in SBP (from baseline to worst on-treatment/follow-up) of \ge 35 mmHg was reported in 0 patients in the tebentafusp arm and 1 patient (0.9%) in the investigator's choice arm; the latter had Grade 3 hypertension at baseline (SBP \ge 160 mmHg).
L1467: Based on reported body temperature measurements, 77 patients (31.4%) in the tebentafusp arm and 108 patients (97.3%) in the investigator's choice arm maintained normal body temperature, 106 (43.3%) and 1 (0.9%) patients, respectively, experienced Grade 1 fever, 56 (22.9%) and 1 (0.9%) patients, respectively, experienced Grade 2 fever, and 6 (2.4%) and 0 patients, respectively, experienced Grade 3 fever; no patient in either treatment arm had Grade 4 fever.
L1468: There were no notable changes between the tebentafusp and investigator's choice arms for shifts from baseline to worst on-treatment/follow-up for weight, pulse rate, and respiratory rate.
L1469: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L1470: FDA agrees. Transient changes in vital signs associated with CRS were frequently observed in patients receiving tebentafusp; however, analysis of vital signs did not identify changes in weight, pulse, or respiratory rate that persisted beyond resolution of CRS.
L1471: ## Electrocardiograms (ECGs)
L1472: ## Data:
L1473: Cardiac AEs related to ECG abnormalities included tachycardia in 2 (10.5%) patients and sinus tachycardia in 1 (5.6%) patient; these events were Grade 1 or 2 in severity, non-serious, and did not result in discontinuation of study drug.
L1474: The most commonly reported (&gt; 1\% of patients) cardiac AEs related to ECG abnormalities were tachycardia (11.8%), sinus tachycardia in (9.4%), sinus bradycardia (3.9%), atrial fibrillation (3.1%), and atrial flutter (2.4%), and supraventricular tachycardia (1.6%). No AEs of torsade's de pointes were reported. Cardiac AEs of Grade \ge 3 severity were reported in 7 patients (5.5%); events related to ECG abnormalities (each in 1 patient, 0.8%) included Grade 3 and Grade 4 atrial fibrillation, atrial flutter, and tachycardia; all were considered treatment-related. Five patients experienced SAEs related to ECG abnormalities.
L1475: In Study 102 and Study 202, there were no clinically meaningful trends in ECG findings and associated cardiac AEs.
L1476: ## The Applicant's Position:
L1477: In Study 102 and Study 202, there were no clinically meaningful trends in ECG findings and associated cardiac AEs.
L1478: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L1479: For more comprehensive discussion of cardiac toxicity see Section 8.2.5.
L1480: Based on review of shifts for PR, QRS, and RR interval abnormalities provided in the CSR for Study 202, FDA agrees with the Applicant. There were no clinically meaningful trends in ECG findings.
L1481: ## QT
L1482: ## **The Applicant's Position:**
L1483: In Study 102 and Study 202, there were no clinically meaningful trends in ECG findings and associated cardiac AEs.
L1484: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L1485: Based on review of the analysis of ECGs provided in the CSR for Study 202, FDA does not find any evidence that tebentafusp is associated with meaningful or persistent QT prolongation. As stated in the CSR, a total of 4 patients (1.6%) in the tebentafusp arm and no patients in the IC arm had post baseline QTcF values &gt; 500 msec. Of the 4 patients in the tebentafusp arm, 3 of them had baseline values ≤ 450 msec and 1 patient had a baseline between 450 and 480 msec. All 4 cases of prolonged QTcF were observed pre-dose (i.e., 1 week from the last dose of tebentafusp) were transient, and returned to &lt; 450 msec at the next time point (i.e., 1 hour post-dose). In addition, 7 patients of patients in the tebentafusp arm (2.9%) and no patients who received IC had an increase from baseline in QTcF &gt; 60 msec. The Applicant states that none of these QTcF changes were associated with clinically significant or reported cardiac disorders. Narratives for patients with post baseline QTcF values &gt; 500 msec were reviewed.
L1486: ## Immunogenicity
L1487: ## **The Applicant's Position:**
L1488: Overall, there was no meaningful impact of ADA on either patient safety or efficacy, based on frequency or grade of hypersensitivity AEs before and after ADA onset. Treatment-emergent ADA were detected in 4% of patients in study IMCgp100-01 using a first-generation assay and limited sampling strategy. In later studies where a more robust assay and sampling strategy were employed, treatment-emergent ADA were detected in 33% and 29% of mUM patients in studies IMCgp100-102 and IMCgp100-202, respectively. In these studies, ADA had a median onset time of 6 (study IMCgp100-102) and 9 weeks (study IMCgp100-202) after the first dose of tebentafusp, and median titers of 8192 (study IMCgp100-102) and 2050 (study IMCgp100-202).
L1489: The majority of these ADA responses were *de novo* treatment-induced responses that persisted after onset. A small number of patients developed high titer ADA (\ge 8192) that resulted in increased tebentafusp clearance and reduced C_{max} exposure. However, there was no clinically relevant impact of ADA on either patient safety or efficacy, based on frequency or grade of hypersensitivity AEs before and after ADA onset, or changes in OS or tumor shrinkage by ADA status or titer, respectively. Thus, monitoring of ADA is not necessary.
L1490: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L1491: See Appendix 19.5. Key conclusions of FDA analysis are:
L1492: - Immunogenicity positive subgroup did not show significant reduced efficacy compared with immunogenicity negative subgroup. However, the result should be considered with caution due to the limited number of patients (N=30) in ADA 8 weeks positive subgroup.
- The high titer immunogenicity positive subgroup did not show significant reduced efficacy compared with the immunogenicity negative or low titer subgroup. However, the result should be considered with caution due to the limited number of patients (N=11) in high titer subgroup.
L1493: ## 8.2.5. Analysis of Submission-Specific Safety Issues
L1494: ## 8.2.5.1 Cytokine Release Syndrome
L1495: ## **Data:**
L1496: To ensure consistency, this section summarizes programmatically derived CRS using the ASTCT 2019 consensus criteria rather than investigator-reported CRS.
L1497: In Study 202, which forms the largest dataset of programmatically derived CRS events, 89% of patients experienced any grade CRS. Most patients had Grade 1 (12%) or Grade 2 (76%) CRS. One patient (0.4%) in the tebentafusp treatment arm had an event of CRS that resulted in discontinuation of study drug. The incidence of Grade 3 CRS was 0.8% (2 patients). There was no Grade 4 CRS or death due to CRS. Common symptoms of CRS included pyrexia (64.9%), hypotension (24.9%) and infrequently hypoxia (0.4%). Other commonly associated symptoms included chills (29%), nausea (19.6%), vomiting (13.5%), fatigue (12.2%), and headache (10.6%).

In Study 202, the maximum Grade 1 - 4 CRS were: 29 (11.84%), 186 (75.92%), 2 (0.82%), and 0 (0%), (respectively).

L1511: Source: Module 5.3.5.3, Table ISS 02.03.08.04.01.
L1512: ## The Applicant's Position:
L1513: CRS is an important identified risk based on the mechanism of action of tebentafusp. CRS was graded based on the ASTCT consensus grading for CRS (Lee, 2019).
L1514: Most cases of CRS occurred approximately within 1 day of tebentafusp infusion and mostly after the first initial doses of tebentafusp. There were too few patients with Grade \ge 3 CRS to reliably interpret a median time to onset or resolution. The incidence and severity decreased with repeated dosing. In the vast majority of cases, CRS started the day of infusion. Pyrexia was noted in nearly all cases of CRS, and in these patients, an increase in body temperature generally occurred within the first 8 to 10 hours after tebentafusp infusion. CRS rarely (1.2%) led to treatment discontinuation. Most cases of CRS of any grade or Grade \ge 2 severity, resolved by the day after most recent tebentafusp infusion.
L1515: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L1516: Cytokine release syndrome (CRS) is an expected class safety issue for biological products that engage T-cells. Drug-induced CRS is a clinical diagnosis based on fever \ge 38^{\circ}\text{C} with or without hypotension and/or hypoxia with a temporal relationship to a treatment with a drug mechanistically known to provoke production of proinflammatory cytokines (Shimabukuro-Vorhagen, 2018). CRS can start within minutes or hours of the first dose and up to 14 days after infusion, depending on the specific drug product.
L1517: Constitutional symptoms (headache, fatigue, arthralgias, myalgias, nausea or rash) are common but not required for diagnosis of CRS. Severe cases may include respiratory failure, hypotension requiring vasopressors, cardiac dysfunction, renal failure, elevated transaminases, and disseminated intravascular coagulation. CRS may result in fatal multiple system organ dysfunction.
L1518: Laboratory findings can include cytopenias, elevated creatinine and liver enzymes, abnormal coagulation parameters, and elevated CRP and ferritin. Laboratory abnormalities can be highly variable and nonspecific and may lag after clinical changes. A diagnosis of CRS does not rely on laboratory findings.
L1519: There are multiple grading systems for CRS in use. Initially, in Study 102 and Study 202, reporting of CRS was based on NCI CTCAE 4.03 criteria, shown in Table 60. In October 2017, the Lee CRS grading criteria (Lee, 2014), shown in Table 61, was incorporated into both studies.
L1520: **Table 60: CTCAE 4.03 Cytokine Release Syndrome Grading Criteria**
L1521: Grade	Symptoms
1	Mild reaction; infusion interruption not indicated intervention not indicated
2	Therapy or infusion interruption indicated but responds promptly to symptomatic treatment (e.g., antihistamines, NSAIDS, narcotics, IV fluids); prophylactic medications indicated for \le 24 hours.
3	Prolonged (e.g., not rapidly responsive to symptomatic medication and/or brief interruption of infusion); recurrence of symptoms following initial improvement; hospitalization indicated for clinical sequelae (e.g., renal impairment, pulmonary infiltrates)
4	Life-threatening consequences; pressor or ventilatory support indicated
5	Death
L1522: **Table 61: Lee Cytokine Release Syndrome Grading Criteria1**
L1523: Grade	Symptoms
1	Symptoms are not life-threatening and require symptomatic treatment only (e.g., fever, nausea, fatigue, headache, myalgia, and malaise)
2	Symptoms require and response to moderate intervention:Oxygen requirement < 40%
Hypotension responsive to fluids or 1 low-dose vasopressor
Grade 2 organ toxicity

3	Symptoms require and response to aggressive intervention:Oxygen requirement > 40%
Hypotension requiring high-dose or multiple vasopressors
Grade 3 organ toxicity
Grade 4 transaminitis

4	Life-threatening symptomsRequirement for ventilator support
Grade 4 organ toxicity (excluding transaminitis)

5	Death
L1524: 1 Lee et. al, 2014
L1525: In Study 202, based on investigator assessed TEAE reporting, 51 patients (21%) had at least one event of CRS, with 20 patients (8%) having a maximum Grade 1 event, 29 patients (12%) having a maximum Grade 2 event, and 2 patients (0.8%) having a maximum Grade 3 event. There were no investigator-reported Grade 4 or 5 CRS events. There were no investigator-reported cases of
L1526: infusion-related reactions. One patient had an event of CRS that resulted in discontinuation of study drug.
L1527: To support a more complete analysis of CRS associated with tebentafusp, the Applicant performed a retrospective analysis based on ASTCT 2019 CRS grading criteria, shown in Table 62. For the analysis, the Application utilized investigator TEAE reporting, vital signs, concomitant medications, and laboratory assessments to identify potential cases of CRS. To identify potential cases of CRS, the Applicant considered the following for each patient infusion:
L1528: - Investigator report of a SAE of any of the following preferred terms on the day of the infusion and up to 2 days afterwards: cytokine release syndrome, cytokine storm, infusion related reaction, pulmonary edema, adverse drug reaction, hypersensitivity reaction, drug hypersensitivity, systemic inflammatory response syndrome, acute distress syndrome event.
- Fever: considered positive if patient had an investigator report of “pyrexia” or “tachycardia” on the day of the infusion or up to 2 days afterwards OR patient received an antipyretic as recorded in the concomitant medications on the day of the infusion and up to 3 days afterwards OR patient had record of maximum temperature \ge 38^{\circ} \text{C} as recorded in the vital signs on the day of or within 2 days of the infusion.
- Hypotension: considered positive if the patient had an investigator report of “hypotension” or “diastolic hypotension” OR systolic blood pressure &lt; 90 \text{ mm/Hg} as recorded in the vital signs on the day of or within 2 days of the infusion.
- Hypoxia: considered positive if the patient had an investigator report of “hypoxia”, “acute respiratory syndrome”, or “dyspnea” on the day of or within 2 days of the infusion OR the patient received oxygen as reported in the concomitant medications on the day of or within 3 days of the infusion.
- Vasopressor use: considered positive if the patient received norepinephrine, epinephrine, dopamine, dobutamine, vasopressin, or phenylephrine as reported in the concomitant medications on the day of or within 3 days of the infusion.
- Steroid use: considered positive if the patient received a corticosteroid for systemic use as reported in the concomitant medications on the day of or within 3 days of the infusion.
- IV Fluid use: considered positive if the patient received fluids via IV as reported in the concomitant medications on the day of or within 3 days of the infusion.
L1529: A spreadsheet of these events was then evaluated by a panel of oncologists experienced with CRS which graded each potential case based on the information provided. Additional information for pre and post vital signs was available. Out of the initial 1127 records of potential cases of CRS in the tebentafusp arm, the panel assessed 805 events occurring in 217 patients as being \ge Grade 1. The remaining records did not meet ASCTC criteria for CRS. Thus, 89% of patients (217/245) in the tebentafusp arm had at least 1 episode of CRS based on an algorithmic adjudication process using ASCTC consensus criteria. The cases comprised 29 patients (12%) with maximum Grade 1 CRS, 186 patients (76%) with maximum Grade 2 CRS, and 2 patients (0.8%) with maximum Grade 3 CRS. There were no Grade \ge 4 events.
L1530: **Table 62: ASCTC 2019 Cytokine Release Syndrome Consensus Grading Criteria1**
L1531: Grade	Symptoms
1	Temperature \ge 38^{\circ} C (not attributable to other cause) with
No hypotension, hypoxia

2	Temperature \ge 38^{\circ} C (not attributable to other cause) with
Hypotension not requiring vasopressors and/or
hypoxia requiring low-flow nasal canula or blow-by

3	Temperature \ge 38^{\circ} C (not attributable to other cause) with
Hypotension requiring a vasopressor with or without vasopressin and/or
hypoxia requiring high-flow nasal canula, facemask, nonrebreather mask, or venturi mask

4	Temperature \ge 38^{\circ} C (not attributable to other cause) with
Hypotension requiring multiple vasopressors (excluding vasopressin) and/or
hypoxia requiring positive pressure (e.g., CPAP, BiPAP, intubation and mechanical ventilation.

This is shown in Figure 41 below.

L1545: **Reviewer Comment:** The actual frequency of CRS in patients receiving tebentafusp is not known. The investigator reporting of 21% of patients with at least one CRS event likely underestimates the frequency, while the ASCTC based Applicant adjudicated calculation of 89% of patients having at least one CRS event likely overestimates the frequency. The true frequency may be closer to the 77% calculation which considers only Grade \ge 2 ASCTC Applicant
L1546: adjudicated CRS. Analysis of only Grade \ge 2 events is reasonable as patients with Grade 1 CRS do not require intervention beyond possible administration of antipyretic.
L1547: While CRS is common in patients receiving tebentafusp, Grade 3 events were rare, and across Studies 202 and 102, there was only 1 case of Grade 4 CRS in which a patient developed multi-organ system failure. While the CRS resolved, the multi-organ system failure persisted and was ongoing at the time the patient died from progressive disease.
L1548: While use of IV fluids on Study 202 was common and may have reflected protocol guidelines which specified IV fluids for Grade 1 hypotension and allowed administration of IV fluids prior to tebentafusp administration, use of oxygen and vasopressor(s) were rare.
L1549: Because CRS has the potential to be a serious and fatal condition, CRS has been included in Section 5 (Warnings and Precautions) and in a Black Box warning in the USPI to inform prescribers of the association between tebentafusp and CRS. The labeling provides information with respect to monitoring and treatment of CRS. Immunocore has also voluntarily developed a communication plan to inform clinicians who will prescribe tebentafusp regarding the risk of CRS.

L1577: Source: Module 5.3.5.3, Table ISS 02.03.08.03.01.
L1578: ## The Applicant's Position:
L1579: Acute skin reactions have been reported with tebentafusp infusion, likely based on its mechanism of action and gp100 expression in normal melanocytes in the skin.
L1580: For all studies combined, 379 patients (92.4%) experienced an event of acute skin reaction. Of the 379 patients, 377 patients (92.0%) reported an acute skin reaction that was considered related to study drug. CTCAE Grade 3 acute skin reaction events considered related to study drug were reported in 86 patients (21.0%). Serious TEAEs of acute skin reactions considered drug related were reported in 18 patients (4.4%). No patients experienced a TEAE of acute skin reaction that led to discontinuation or death. TEAEs (PTs) of acute skin reaction with the highest incidence (\ge 10% of patients in tebentafusp arm) were pruritus (71.2%), rash (50.2%), rash maculo-papular (32.0%), erythema (25.6%), skin exfoliation (21.0%), and periorbital oedema (19.5%).
L1581: Acute skin reactions typically occurred following each of the first three infusions, with decreasing severity and frequency. The median time to onset of acute skin reactions was 1 day in the tebentafusp treated patients and median time to improvement to \le Grade 1 was 6 days. There were no discontinuations of tebentafusp due to acute skin reactions. Majority of symptoms resolved without any systemic corticosteroid or any long term sequelae. No cases of Stevens-Johnson syndrome or toxic epidermal necrolysis were reported.
L1582: Acute skin reactions were managed with antihistamine and topical corticosteroids. Systemic steroids were used for persistent or severe symptoms.
L1583: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L1584: Acute skin reactions associated with tebentafusp include the subcategories of rash, pruritus, pigment change, erythema, edema, dry skin, and other changes.
L1585: The list of PTs for the composite term rash used by the Applicant Study 202 included blister, dermatitis, dermatitis acneiform, dermatitis allergic, dermatitis bullous, dermatitis contact, dermatitis exfoliative, dermatosis, drug eruption, eczema, eczema eyelids, erythema multiforme, excoriation, exfoliative rash, interstitial granulomatous dermatitis, lichenification, lichenoid keratosis, palmar-plantar erythrodysaesthesia syndrome, papule, prurigo, psoriasis, rash, rash erythematous, rash generalised, rash macular, rash maculo-papular, rash papular, rash pruritic, rash vesicular, seborrhoea, seborrheic dermatitis, skin abrasion, skin erosion, skin exfoliation, skin irritation, skin plaque, solar dermatitis, toxic skin eruption, and urticaria.
L1586: FDA agrees with the composition of the Applicant's group term rash. FDA validated the frequency for the AESI skin toxicity and the individual component PTs for Study 202 without regard to investigator assigned causality. Table 67 summarizes the frequency of rash (composite term) by grade for Study 202. There were no Grade \ge 4 events. In the tebentafusp arm, 12 patients (4.9%) had AESI of rash that were considered SAEs, 4 patients (1.6%) had AESI

L1593: **Reviewer Comment:** FDA agrees with the Applicant's assessment of skin toxicity. Although common, skin toxicity did not result in treatment discontinuation or fatalities. While 3 patients (1.2%) had TEAEs of erythema multiforme, 2 were Grade 1 and 1 was Grade 2. There were no cases of Stevens-Johnson Syndrome or Drug Rash with Eosinophilia and Systemic Symptoms (DRESS). Due to the frequency of events, Skin Reactions were included in Section 5 (Warnings and Precautions) of the USPI.
L1594: ## 8.2.5.3 Liver Function Test Evaluations
L1595: ## The Applicant's Position:
L1596: LFT elevations are not uncommon in mUM since the liver is the predominant site of hematogenous spread from uveal melanoma. Tumor progression within the liver can result in liver injury with a rise in LFTs (Diener-West 2004, Eskelin 1999).
L1597: Hepatocytes do not express gp100 and tebentafusp did not redirect T cells against normal hepatocytes in nonclinical in vitro studies. However, treatment with tebentafusp may result in transient secondary effects from tebentafusp-induced inflammation in mUM liver lesions, including either activated immune cells (e.g., T cells) or cytokines.
L1598: Due to the number of patients enrolled, optimal schedule of laboratory collection and comparative AE and laboratory data, Study 202 provides the best reference for characterization of changes of laboratory values over time and reporting of adverse events.
L1599: In Study 202, based on investigator reported target and non-target lesions, greater than 95% of patients had baseline liver metastases and nearly two thirds of tebentafusp treated patients (158/245; 64.5%) and approximately half of investigator choice treated patients (53/111; 47.7%) had an increase in post-baseline grade of ALT/AST. Although numerically higher, the majority of ALT/AST elevations in the tebentafusp treatment arm were mild, occurred early, and did not result in treatment discontinuation. Since most patients had pre-existing liver metastases, LFT elevations may be secondary to T cell redirection into liver metastases.
L1600: No deaths due to ALT/AST elevations were observed and more than 90% of patients were able to continue treatment beyond worst grade ALT/AST elevation. Most patients experiencing Grade 3 or 4 ALT/AST elevations had improvement to \le Grade 1 within 7 days. Elevations in bilirubin have been reported in 27% of patients and these were primarily associated with increase in size of liver metastasis.
L1601: Across all studies, patients with largest liver lesion \ge 3 cm or values &gt; upper limit of normal (ULN) for LDH and alkaline phosphatase (ALP) at baseline were at higher risk of developing elevated LFTs during treatment. However, this may have been due to the poor prognostic nature of these baseline variables and that these patients are more likely to have disease progression of their liver lesions as a likely cause of the on-treatment LFT increase.
L1602: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L1603: The Applicant identified cases of hepatotoxicity using the Standardized MedDRA Query (SMQ, Broad and narrow) Drug related hepatic disorders. Table 68 summarizes drug related hepatic disorders by grade for Study 202. In the tebentafusp arm, 11 patients (4.4%) had hepatotoxicity AESIs, as identified by SMQ, that were SAEs. In the tebentafusp arm, hepatotoxicity AESIs, as identified by SMQ, resulted in withholding of study drug in 13 (5%) of patients and permanent discontinuation of study treatment in 1 patient (0.4%).
L1604: Worsening laboratory assessments from baseline are presented in Table 57. While the majority of patients on the tebentafusp arm had a worsening of AST and/or ALT, only 12% and 8% respectively worsened to Grade 3, and only 0.4% and 0.8% of patients respectively had maximum toxicity Grade 4.

L1607: *Reviewer Comment:* Abnormalities in liver tests were common in patients receiving tebentafusp. This may have been due to CRS or underlying liver metastases. Overall, however, the abnormalities were mild and did not require discontinuation of study treatment. However, due to the frequency of events and the potential for serious outcomes Elevated Liver Enzymes were included in Section 5 (Warnings and Precautions) of the USPI.
L1608: ## 8.2.5.4 Eye Disorders
L1609: ## Data:
L1610: Patients with UM have baseline recurrent or persistent local ocular events relating to their primary treatment (e.g., enucleation, complication of RT, intravitreal anti-VEGF therapy). In addition, eye disorders were initially identified as a risk since gp100 positive intra-ocular melanocytes could serve as a target for tebentafusp which in theory may lead to uveitis or vision changes. As a result, detailed baseline and on-study ophthalmologic exams were incorporated into Study 01.
L1611: No concerning or related intra-ocular clinical findings or overt vision changes were identified per detailed ophthalmologic exams. Most reported eye AEs were extra-ocular and due to inflammation or peri-orbital skin AEs, including periorbital oedema (41 patients, 48.8%), eye pain (3 patients, 3.6%), and eye pruritus (3 patients, 3.6%). All reported ophthalmologic AEs were of CTCAE Grade 1 or 2. The periorbital AEs are consistent with tebentafusp redirecting T cells to gp100-positive melanocytes in the skin, do not impact vision, are reversible and have been observed in tebentafusp treated patients in all studies.
L1612: In Study 202, a total of 79 patients (32.2%) in the tebentafusp arm and 15 patients (13.5%) in the investigator's choice arm had events in the SOC of eye disorders. Events with the highest incidence (\ge 3% of patients in either the tebentafusp or investigator's choice arms) were periorbital edema (10.6% vs 0.9%) and lacrimation increased (3.3% vs 1.8%). Grade \ge 3 events were reported in 3 patients (1.2%) in the tebentafusp arm and 2 patients (1.8%) in the investigator's choice arm and included cataract, eye pain, glaucoma (0.4% vs 0% each), retinal
L1613: detachment and uveitis (0% vs 0.9% each). Events were considered treatment-related by the investigator in 60 patients (24.5%) in the tebentafusp arm and 6 patients (5.4%) in the investigator's choice arm. Treatment-related events of Grade \ge 3 severity were reported in 2 patients (0.8%) and 1 patient (0.9%), respectively; these events were cataract and eye pain in the tebentafusp arm and uveitis in the investigator's choice arm.
L1614: ## **The Applicant's Position:**
L1615: Serious eye disorders were reported in 2 patients (0.8%) in the tebentafusp arm and 1 patient (0.9%) in the investigator's choice arm who received pembrolizumab. The SAEs were diplopia (Patient [REDACTED](b) (6)) and periorbital edema (Patient [REDACTED](b) (6)) in the tebentafusp arm and uveitis (Patient [REDACTED](b) (6)) in the investigator's choice arm. All SAEs were considered treatment-related and the events of periorbital edema and uveitis resolved. None of the events in the eye disorders SOC resulted in study drug discontinuation.
L1616: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L1617: FDA agrees with the Applicant's assessment of eye disorders.
L1618: ## **8.2.5.5 Cardiac Disorders**
L1619: ## **Data:**
L1620: In Study 102 in the Phase 1 dose escalation cohorts, cardiac disorders (regardless of causality) were reported in 4 (21.1%) patients. Cardiac AEs related to ECG abnormalities included tachycardia in 2 (10.5%) patients and sinus tachycardia in 1 (5.6%) patient; these events were Grade 1 or 2 in severity, non-serious, and did not result in discontinuation of study drug.
L1621: In Study 102 in the Phase 2 dose expansion cohort, cardiac disorders (regardless of causality) were reported in 38 (29.9%) patients. The most commonly reported (&gt; 1% of patients) cardiac AEs related to ECG abnormalities were tachycardia (11.8%), sinus tachycardia in (9.4%), sinus bradycardia (3.9%), atrial fibrillation (3.1%), and atrial flutter (2.4%), and supraventricular tachycardia (1.6%). No AEs of torsades de pointes were reported. Cardiac AEs of Grade \ge 3 severity were reported in 7 patients (5.5%); events related to ECG abnormalities (each in 1 patient, 0.8%) included Grade 3 and Grade 4 atrial fibrillation, atrial flutter, and tachycardia; all were considered treatment-related. Two patients experienced cardiac AEs that led to treatment discontinuation: one patient with grade 4 atrial fibrillation after day 1 of study treatment [Patient [REDACTED](b) (6)] and a second patient with grade 3 left ventricular dysfunction after day 8 of study treatment [Patient [REDACTED](b) (6)]. Both of these cardiac AEs occurred in the setting of sponsor assessed Grade 3 CRS and were considered related to tebentafusp treatment by the investigator. No events of Grade 5 severity were reported. Five patients experienced SAEs related to ECG abnormalities.
L1622: In Study 202, a total of 37 patients (15.1%) in the tebentafusp arm and 8 patients (7.2%) in the
L1623: investigator's choice arm had events in the SOC of cardiac disorders. The event with the highest incidence (\ge 3\% of patients in either the tebentafusp or investigator's choice arms) was tachycardia (9.8% vs 2.7%). Events were considered treatment-related by the investigator in 27 patients (11.0%) in the tebentafusp arm and 3 patients (2.7%) in the investigator's choice arm. All treatment-related events were Grade 1 or 2 in severity and included tachycardia (9.0% vs 0%), sinus tachycardia (2.4% vs 0.9%), atrioventricular block, bradycardia (0.4% vs 0% each), and palpitations (0% vs 1.8%). There were no clinically meaningful trends in ECG findings and associated cardiac AEs in either treatment arm.
L1624: ## The Applicant's Position:
L1625: During the pre-BLA administrative meeting, the US FDA requested that along with QT assessment, narratives for patients with arrhythmias, syncope, or with QTcF values &gt; 500 msec should be provided with the BLA submission.
L1626: Given tebentafusp induces mild to moderate CRS, not surprisingly, the most frequent cardiac AEs were pyrexia, hypotension and tachycardia. Due to the anticipated risk of hypotension, Studies 102 and 202 required patients with pre-existing hypertension to temporarily suspend anti-hypertensive medications prior to the first few doses. Therefore, hypertension resulting from interruption of daily anti-hypertensives was occasionally observed during the first few weeks of treatment and reported as an AE. This hypertension is considered an iatrogenic AE and distinct from the mechanism of action of tebentafusp.
L1627: Serious cardiac disorders were reported in 0 patients in the tebentafusp arm and 1 patient (0.9%) in the investigator's choice arm. The patient in the investigator's choice arm who received dacarbazine (Patient (b) (6)) had a serious event of left ventricular dysfunction that resulted in study drug discontinuation and death; the event was considered as not related to study drug. Besides the event of left ventricular dysfunction on the investigator choice arm, none of the other events in the cardiac disorders SOC resulted in study drug discontinuation.
L1628: In Study 102 and Study 202, there were no clinically meaningful trends in ECG findings and associated cardiac AEs.
L1629: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L1630: FDA agrees with the Applicant's assessment of cardiac toxicity.
L1631: ## 8.2.5.6 Neurotoxicity
L1632: ## The FDA's Assessment
L1633: Neurotoxicity, including potentially life-threatening or fatal events, is an identified complication associated with T-cell engager therapies, including anti-CD19 CAR-T cells, bi-specific T cell engagers (Zheng et. al., 2019). Although neurotoxicity was not considered an AESI for Study 202
L1634: ## NDA/BLA Multi-disciplinary Review and Evaluation BLA 761228
## Kimtrak (tebentafusp)
L1635: based on prior clinical experience, based on mechanism of action, FDA performed a separate assessment of the risks of neurotoxicity for Study 202.
L1636: The table below shows the frequency of neurotoxicities, which includes all PTs in the SOC "Nervous System Disorders", with the exclusion of terms clearly related to structural pathology e.g., carpal tunnel syndrome, cerebral cyst, CSR fluid circulation disorder, intracranial aneurysm, intracranial mass, perineurial cyst, sciatica, spinal cord compression.
L1637: Neurotoxicities were frequent on Study 202. A higher percentage of patients in the tebentafusp arm (51%) had at least one neurotoxicity TEAE, any grade, compared to 24% in the IC arm; however, Grade 3-4 events were rare on either arm with only 2.4% of patients in the tebentafusp arm having at least one Grade 3 or 4 event. There were no fatal neurotoxicity TEAEs on either arm. In the tebentafusp arm, 4 patients (1.6%) had a neurotoxicity SAE while in the IC arm, 1 patient (0.9%) had a neurotoxicity SAE in the ICC arm. The SAEs in the tebentafusp arm were motor dysfunction, dizziness, brain edema, and presyncope.

L1667: ## 8.2.8. Specific Safety Studies/Clinical Trials
L1668: ## Data:
L1669: Study 401 was a Phase 2, open-label, multi-center rollover study conducted to provide continued access to tebentafusp treatment for patients enrolled in a completed clinical study (Study 01) of tebentafusp. A total of 3 patients, all from the Study 01 parent study, were enrolled in this rollover study. All 3 patients were white, 2 were male and 1 was female, and their age ranged from 49 to 71 years. All patients had advanced melanoma with stage IV disease. Patients received 2, 18, and 26 cycles of tebentafusp with treatment durations of 43, 505, and 728 days. In
L1670: total, treatment durations from the first dose in the parent study were 423, 960, and 1156 days. No unexpected safety findings were observed in the 3 treated patients despite the additional exposure.
L1671: ## The Applicant's Position:
L1672: Following long-term exposure, the safety profile of tebentafusp was tolerable, manageable, and consistent with observations from other tebentafusp studies.
L1673: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L1674: Study 401 was not evaluated as part of FDA's assessment of safety as there were too few patients to provide meaningful additional safety information.
L1675: ## 8.2.9. **Additional Safety Explorations**
L1676: ## Human Carcinogenicity or Tumor Development
L1677: ## The Applicant's Position:
L1678: Not applicable
L1679: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L1680: Carcinogenicity studies were not indicated and have not been conducted with tebentafusp.
L1681: ## Human Reproduction and Pregnancy
L1682: ## Data:
L1683: ## **See Reproductive and Developmental Toxicology**
L1684: ## The Applicant's Position:
L1685: Due to the specificity for human targets, embryo-fetal development (EFD) studies cannot be conducted in nonclinical species. Therefore, a weight of evidence assessment was conducted to evaluate the risk of tebentafusp administration on pregnancy and embryo-fetal development. A literature search revealed that gp100 is expressed embryonically in melanocytes found in the skin, inner ear, and eyes by 7 weeks of gestation, and MHC Class I classical proteins (including HLA-A*02:01) and transporter-associated-with-antigen-processing (TAP) proteins required for presentation of antigen on the cell surface are expressed very early in embryonic development. In addition, CD3-positive T cells begin to populate the periphery and are capable of being activated by around 12 weeks of gestation.
L1686: However, tebentafusp's ability to directly affect embryo-fetal development requires that tebentafusp crosses the placenta and gains access to the fetus. The molecular weight of tebentafusp is 77 kDa, therefore, it is much too large to cross the placenta by diffusion. It also does not contain an Fc domain and, therefore, cannot bind to the neonatal Fc receptor (FcRn), which facilitates transfer of IgG molecules across the placenta, nor does it have domains that
L1687: could bind to other placental transporters. Thus, tebentafusp would not be able to gain access to or elicit pharmacologic activity directly in the embryo/fetus. There is also no evidence that tebentafusp could interfere with implantation or the maintenance of pregnancy through its mechanism of action.
L1688: Based on the results of this weight of evidence assessment, tebentafusp would not be expected to adversely affect embryo-fetal development or the maintenance of pregnancy when administered during pregnancy.
L1689: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L1690: FDA does not agree. There is data that biologics with a similar mechanism of action and molecular weight cross the placenta. See Section 5.1 of this review. The labeling was therefore amended to include in Warnings and Precautions (new Section 5.4 of KIMMTRAK Prescribing Information) information that there is a risk that tebentafusp may cause harm when administered to a pregnant woman. See Section 11.
L1691: ## **Pediatrics and Assessment of Effects on Growth**
L1692: ## **Data:**
L1693: Not applicable.
L1694: ## **The Applicant's Position:**
L1695: Safety and efficacy of tebentafusp have not been established in pediatric patients. Tebentafusp has received FDA agreement for the initial pediatric study plan for tebentafusp on 23 March 2021. The Sponsor has submitted a request for full pediatric waiver for tebentafusp to the BLA based on nonrelevance of gp100 as a molecular target for any pediatric cancers.
L1696: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L1697: The Applicant did not submit any data regarding safety in pediatric population.
L1698: ## **Overdose, Drug Abuse Potential, Withdrawal, and Rebound**
L1699: ## **Data:**
L1700: There has been no experience of overdosage with tebentafusp or evidence of drug abuse with tebentafusp from clinical studies.
L1701: ## **The Applicant's Position:**
L1702: There is no specific treatment for tebentafusp overdose and symptoms of overdose have not been established. Tebentafusp is unlikely to be abused due to its mode of action, physiological and pharmacological activity, lack of stimulant properties, and clinical setting of use. Because tebentafusp has no known potential for dependence, withdrawal or rebound events are not
L1703: relevant to tebentafusp dosing.
L1704: **The FDA's Assessment:**
FDA agrees with the Applicant's assessment.

L1716: ## 8.2.11. Integrated Assessment of Safety
L1717: **Data:**
See the below section for the integrated assessment of safety summary.
L1718: ## The Applicant's Position:
L1719: Overall, tebentafusp has a well-established safety profile across the 5 Immunocore-sponsored clinical studies in 587 patients with melanoma, including 410 patients with UM, who were exposed to tebentafusp monotherapy. The randomized Study 202 provides the primary safety data and is proposed to be the basis for labeling presentation of Warning and Precautions and Adverse Events sections of the USPI. While the Study 102 safety data are consistent with Study 202 and supportive of the proposed indication and dosing regimen.
L1720: The safety profile of tebentafusp in mUM in Study 202 and Study 102 Phase 2 expansion was consistent with that observed in the pooled UM and CM populations, and no new safety signals were identified. The rate of permanent study drug discontinuation due to tebentafusp-related AEs was low in both studies (2.0% and 2.4%, respectively), and there were no treatment-related deaths among the 372 patients treated with tebentafusp in these two studies.
L1721: Overall, the most frequent TEAEs were associated with the mechanism of action of tebentafusp. These included skin AEs (e.g., rash, pruritus, and erythema), presumably resulting from T cells redirected to gp100+ melanocytes in the skin, and cytokine-mediated AEs, due to T cell activation. The most clinically relevant cytokine mediated AE is CRS, which is summarized using the most recent, and widely used, ASTCT 2019 consensus grading criteria. The proposed management algorithm for acute skin reactions and for CRS were based on those used in Study 102 and Study 202.
L1722: Most CRS events were mild to moderate with a low frequency of severe events, despite no requirement for primary prophylaxis with corticosteroid, antihistamine, or acetaminophen. The monitoring and management algorithm for rash and CRS used in the Phase 2 and 3 programs were successful based on low rate of drug related discontinuation, no drug related deaths and striking overall survival (OS) benefit. The majority of these AEs had onset within 1 day after the first 3 doses of tebentafusp when the patient was undergoing extended monitoring for at least 16 hours. As has been observed for other T cell engaging bispecifics, the CRS underwent tachyphylaxis with repeated dosing.
L1723: In first line mUM patients in Study 202, tebentafusp significantly improved OS compared to investigator's choice in the ITT analysis. The strong association between rash in Week 1 and OS benefit from tebentafusp suggests that rash may be a marker that the immune system can be mobilized by tebentafusp to target gp100+ cells. However, rash should not be used for clinical management since most patients develop a rash, rash was not independent of baseline prognostic features, and patients without a rash may still have better OS than similar patients in the control arm.
L1724: The other notable TEAE was LFT elevations, which were observed in both arms of Study 202 although with higher incidence in the tebentafusp arm (i.e., nearly two-thirds of patients). Liver function test elevation is a common feature of mUM since &gt; 95% of patients had pre-existing liver metastases. The higher incidence of LFTs following tebentafusp, which occurred in a similar time frame to both cytokine increase and T cell trafficking to the liver tumors, may be due to spill over cellular inflammation from the UM lesions in the liver and injury to adjacent
L1725: hepatocytes or due to elevated cytokines. Elevations in LFTs typically occurred after the first few doses of tebentafusp and generally were transient and manageable with treatment interruption alone. No safety signal was observed in cardiac or eye disorders that would require specific labeling.
L1726: In a subset analysis, a trend for a higher risk of elevated LFTs was observed where patients with largest liver lesion &gt;3 cm, elevated LDH, or elevated ALP at baseline. However, this may have that these patients are more likely to have disease progression of their liver lesions as a likely cause of the on-treatment LFT increase. In addition, these baseline factors are also known poor prognostic features in mUM.
L1727: mUM is a life-threatening disease with a dismal prognosis. There are currently no effective treatments for mUM that extend survival, and no standard of care exists. Tebentafusp is a first-in-class bispecific gp100-targeted TCR fusion protein that can redirect polyclonal T cells to target gp100+ tumor cells leading to T cell activation and release of inflammatory mediators. Tebentafusp monotherapy provides a dramatic OS benefit in HLA*A*02:01-positive patients with mUM. The safety profile of tebentafusp administered at the proposed dosing regimen is acceptable, with a very low rate of treatment-related discontinuations and no treatment-related deaths. AEs are consistent with mechanism of action of tebentafusp, predictable, generally reversible, and clinically manageable with appropriate surveillance and intervention. The totality of the data supports a positive benefit-risk profile of tebentafusp as a novel treatment option for HLA*A*02:01-positive adult patients with unresectable or mUM.
L1728: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L1729: The evaluation of the safety of tebentafusp at the proposed dosage in patients with metastatic uveal melanoma was based primarily on 356 patients randomized in Study 202 who received at least one dose of study drug (tebentafusp: 245 patients; IC: 111 patients). The Applicant included in their portion of the safety review analysis of data from 2 supportive trials (Study 01, Study 102 Phase 1 cohorts, Study 102 Phase 2 cohorts) and a pooled analysis (N= 410) comprised of all patients from these studies and the 245 patients who received at least one dose of tebentafusp on Study 202. FDA's assessment of safety included an analysis using a pooled set of 505 patients with cutaneous or uveal melanoma who received at least 1 dose of tebentafusp monotherapy on a clinical study (Study 01: 84 patients; Study 102: N= 146; Study 201: N=30; Study 202: N=245).
L1730: Study 202 excluded patients &lt; 18 years of age, with a serum creatinine &gt; 1.5 \times ULN and/or creatinine clearance (calculating using Cockcroft-Gault formula, or measured) &lt; 50 mL/minute, clinically significant cardiac disease or impaired cardiac function, history of HIV infection, active HBV or HCV infection, or history of adrenal insufficiency, interstitial lung disease, pneumonitis that required corticosteroid treatment or current pneumonitis, history of colitis of inflammatory bowel disease.
L1731: In Study 202, the median exposure was 163 days (range 1 day to 1016 days) in the tebentafusp
L1732: arm compared to 65 days (range 1 day to 658 days) in the IC arm. Table 71 summarizes the overall safety profile of tebentafusp at the proposed dosage as observed on Study 202.
L1733: Overall, tebentafusp was well tolerated with a very low rate of fatal TEAEs in Study 202 and across the development program, with no deaths identified as likely related to study drug based on review of available narratives and available data. The percentage of patients receiving tebentafusp on Study 202 who had at least one SAE was high but comparable to that observed with the IC. Over 50% of patients receiving tebentafusp on Study 202 had at least one Grade 3-4 TEAE. This was higher than observed in the IC arm. While SAEs and Grade 3-5 TEAEs were common in patients in the tebentafusp arm in Study 202, they rarely lead to permanent discontinuation, which was infrequent at 3.3%. A significant percentage of patients receiving tebentafusp had at least TEAE that lead to treatment interruption; however, this was comparable to that observed on the IC arm.
L1734: TEAE analysis of the ISS_Pool yielded similar results to what was seen in the tebentafusp arm for Study 202 with the exception of SAEs which was 8% higher in the ISS_Pool. The lower rate in Study 202 may represent improved management guidelines for CRS provided in Study 202.
