L1737: 1 ASCTC Criteria Applicant Adjudicated
L1738: 2 Applicant Adjudication applied only to Studies 202 and 102
L1739: Adverse reactions associated with tebentafusp are primarily those associated with CRS including pyrexia, chills, nausea, vomiting, abdominal pain, hypotension, and those associated with skin reactions considered related to tebentafusp's mechanism of action. These skin reactions include rash, dry skin, and pruritis. Worsening laboratory abnormalities, including both hematologic, metabolic, and chemistry assessments were common and associated with CRS. They were usually transient. Only for lymphocyte count decreased was the percentage of patients with Grade 4 laboratory abnormality \ge 5%.
L1740: The most important adverse reaction associated with tebentafusp is CRS. While there were no fatalities identified across the development program considered related to CRS, there were 2 cases of Grade 4 CRS associated events (0.8%) in the ISS_Pool, both being cases of multiple system organ dysfunction. In Study 202, the most commonly administered management interventions for CRS were IV fluids and steroids. Vasopressor(s) were rarely required.
L1741: Other than the rare and life-threatening CSR related event of multiple system organ dysfunction noted above, analysis of the ISS_Pool dataset did not identify any new or more serious safety already identified in Study 202.
L1742: The safety profile of tebentafusp at the proposed dosage in patient with advanced or metastatic uveal melanoma who have not received prior systemic therapy in the metastatic setting is acceptable with most adverse reactions being Grade 1 or 2 and higher grade toxicities adequately managed through temporary medical intervention or treatment interruption.
L1743: ## SUMMARY AND CONCLUSIONS
L1744: ## 8.3. Statistical Issues
L1745: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L1746: No major statistical issues were identified while reviewing the data and results submitted in this application. Note that despite the statistically significant results observed for PFS, FDA considers the difference in PFS observed between the treatment arms was not clinically meaningful. ORR analysis was not formally tested at this interim look.
L1747: ## 8.4. Conclusions and Recommendations
L1748: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L1749: FDA concludes that the results of Study 202 demonstrate that tebentafusp provides a statistically significant and clinically meaningful improvement in overall survival in patients who were randomized to receive tebentafusp compared to patients randomized to receive Investigator's Choice therapy. Overall survival is considered by FDA to be an acceptable endpoint to demonstrate the effectiveness of new therapeutics across oncology indications. Study 202 provides the evidence that supports the effectiveness of tebentafusp in patients with metastatic uveal melanoma who have not received prior systemic therapy in the metastatic setting. The safety profile of tebentafusp at the proposed recommended dosage of 20 mcg on Day 1, 30 mcg on Day 8, 68 mcg on Day 15, and 68 mcg once every week thereafter is acceptable in the context of patients with a serious and life-threatening condition and limited treatment options and is manageable with guidelines provided in product labeling. Overall, the review team deems the benefit: risk assessment of tebentafusp in the indicated population to be favorable and recommends regular approval for the following indication:
L1750: *KIMTRACK is a bispecific peptide-HLA-directed CD3 T cell engager indicated for the treatment of HLA-A*02:01-positive adult patients with unresectable or metastatic uveal melanoma.*

L1777: ## 12 Risk Evaluation and Mitigation Strategies (REMS)
L1778: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L1779: The risks of tebentafusp are acceptable in the indicated patient population with a serious and life-threatening condition; the safe use of tebentafusp can be adequately implemented in the post-marketing setting through product labeling that includes a black box warning for the risk of CRS. FDA requested the Applicant provide a plan to further communicate the risk of CRS to health care providers beyond product labeling though the distribution of a Dear Health Care Provider Letter. The Applicant agreed with this request and has provided the letter and a plan to distribute the letter to all health care providers expected to prescribe and manage treatment of tebentafusp outside the clinical trial setting.
L1780: ## 13 Postmarketing Requirements and Commitment
L1781: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L1782: The review team recommends issuing the following postmarketing requirement (PRMs) and postmarketing commitments (PMCs)
L1783: ## Clinical Pharmacology PMC:
L1784: - Conduct an assessment to determine the presence of neutralizing anti-drug antibodies against tebentafusp-tebn in all patient samples that tested positive for binding anti-drug antibodies in studies IMCgp100-202 and IMCgp100-102 by using the validated neutralizing anti-drug antibody assay. Include an assessment of the clinical impact of the neutralizing anti-drug antibodies on pharmacokinetics, efficacy and safety of tebentafusp-tebn in the study report.
L1785: ## Clinical PMC:
L1786: - Conduct an assay validation study to demonstrate that the assay can accurately differentiate HLA-A*02:01 from other HLA A alleles for identification of patients with uveal melanoma who may benefit from treatment with Kimmtrak (tebentafusp-tebn) and use the data to support an FDA approved companion diagnostic HLA typing assay patient selection test.

L1793: ## 17 Division Director (Clinical)
L1794: I agree with the clinical, statistical, non-clinical, pharmacology, and CMC recommendations with respect to the approvability of BLA 761228 as well as for the demonstrated favorable risk-benefit assessment for tebentafusp-tebn.
L1795: Tebentafusp-tebn (referred to as tebentafusp) is a bispecific biological product designed to bind to gp100 peptide presented by human leukocyte antigen-A*02:01 (HLA-A*02:01) on the cell surface of uveal melanoma tumor cells. Tebentafusp exerts its anti-tumor effect by redirecting T cells to attack the gp100-expressing tumor cells. The applicant for this BLA (Immunocore) seeks marketing authorization for tebentafusp for the treatment of HLA-A*02:01-positive adult patients with unresectable or metastatic uveal melanoma. The proposed dosing regimen is 20 mcg on Day 1, 30 mcg on Day 8, 68 mcg on Day 15, and 68 mcg once every week thereafter, via intravenous infusion over 15-20 minutes.
L1796: The epidemiology of uveal melanoma is described above in this review. Briefly, Immunocore submitted data from one randomized clinical trial, Study 202, to support the approval of tebentafusp. Study 202 demonstrated a clinically meaningful and statistically significant improvement in overall survival (OS) in patients who received tebentafusp compared to those who received investigator's choice of therapy which included dacarbazine, pembrolizumab, or ipilimumab [HR of 0.51 (95% CI: 0.37, 0.71), p &lt; 0.0001; with a median OS of 21.7 (18.6, 28.6) months in the tebentafusp arm versus 16.0 (9.7, 18.4) months in the control arm]. The results were robust (e.g., p value less than 0.01) and clinically significant and therefore consistent with FDA's requirement to demonstrate substantial evidence of effectiveness. Furthermore, I believe it would not be feasible or ethical to require a second randomized clinical trial, in this very rare malignancy without effective available therapies, prior to approval.
L1797: The adverse event profile of tebentafusp is extensively described above in this review. CRS, dermatological, and hepatic toxicities are important adverse reactions related to tebentafusp. Although important and potentially life threatening, the effect observed on overall survival supports a favorable risk-benefit assessment for the overall population of HLA-A*02:01-positive adult patients with unresectable or metastatic uveal melanoma.
L1798: Tebentafusp will be prescribed by oncologists who are trained in the monitoring and management of drugs known to cause life threatening toxicities (including CRS). Risk minimization strategies include a Black Box Warning for CRS, specific instructions in labeling for monitoring and management, and a Medication Guide. In addition, health care providers that will be responsible for prescribing or managing patients receiving tebentafusp will receive a "Dear Health Care Provider" letter to increase awareness of the risk of CRS. The OMEPRM review provides an extensive discussion of REMS considerations; differences between
L1799: tebentafusp and blinatumomab (which has a REMS); and that a REMS was not considered necessary to ensure the benefits of tebentafusp outweigh its risks.
L1800: Finally, although tebentafusp will be indicated for HLA-A*02:01-positive adult patients, at the time of approval, there will not be an FDA approved/cleared in vitro companion diagnostic device to select patients for treatment. Although not available, HLA typing has been conducted for years in clinical practice (with accurate results necessary for allogeneic stem cell transplants and certain solid organ transplants). Nevertheless, Immunocore has committed, via a PMC, to conduct an assay validation study to demonstrate that the assay can accurately differentiate HLA-A*02:01 from other HLA A alleles. This PMC is intended to support the availability of an in vitro companion diagnostic device. Importantly, the final PMC report is expected in June of 2022. Although contemporaneous approval of a companion diagnostic device is preferable, given the circumstances of this application (including rare disease, high mortality, and observed clinical effects), I do not believe that approval of tebentafusp should be delayed for about 6 (or more) months while waiting for the approval of the device.

L1844: ## 19.2. Financial Disclosure
L1845: ## The Applicant's Position:
L1846: Studies IMCgp100-102 and IMCgp100-202 are the two covered clinical studies. There is nothing to declare for financial interest by any investigator. At the time of conduct, data collection and analysis of these studies Immunocore was not public; therefore, there is no conflict to disclose. All financial support for the covered studies were for cost to conduct the studies and tracked by Immunocore's financial function.
L1847: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L1848: FDA notes there may be conflict of interest outside of public trading; however, given that all financial support was for cost to conduct the studies, FDA agrees there was no reportable conflicts of interest in the conduct of the two studies.
L1849: FDA has split the form filled out by the applicant into separate forms, one for each covered study.

L1862: ## 19.4. Nonclinical Pharmacology/Toxicology
L1863: ## The Applicant's Position:
L1864: Tebentafusp can only recognize human HLA; therefore, all nonclinical toxicology studies were conducted in vitro. Human tissue and cell-based in vitro approaches were taken to understand potential organ-specific toxicities that may arise in the clinical setting with tebentafusp. These
L1865: approaches considered the on-target/off-tumor potential effects, as well as the off-target/off-tumor effects (cross-reactivity) of tebentafusp.
L1866: Tebentafusp was also investigated in toxicology studies to assess its cross-reactivity with normal tissues in a GLP-compliant tissue cross-reactivity study. The ability of tebentafusp to bind to a panel of 35 normal human tissues from 3 healthy donors was assessed using immunohistochemistry (IHC). Staining with tebentafusp was observed in the membrane and cytoplasm of lymphocytes throughout the human tissue panel examined. Similar staining was observed with the individual anti-CD3 component of tebentafusp, while no staining was observed with the individual TCR component, indicating that this staining was attributable to the binding of tebentafusp to CD3. As CD3 is a T lymphocyte marker, this staining was expected. No other staining was observed across the panel of tissues tested, indicating no unexpected cross-reactivity. It should be noted that the TCR targeting domain of tebentafusp recognizes very low target pHLA complexes (&gt;5 pHLA complexes per cell), which are expected to be outside the normal range of sensitivity of the IHC method and would therefore inform on cross-reactivity above the threshold of detection of IHC only. Overall, the results of this study are consistent with published reports of gp100 being specific to melanocytes and melanoma cells, and not in other.
L1867: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L1868: FDA generally agrees with the Applicant's position. See FDA's Assessment in Section 5.5.1.
L1869: ## 19.5. OCP Appendices (Technical documents supporting OCP recommendations)
L1870: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L1871: ## Basic characteristics of immunogenicity:
L1872: - Anti-drug antibodies (ADA) positive rate is 28.6% (63/225 patients) in study IMCgp100-202 and 33.3% (48/144) in study IMCgp100-102.
- Median ADA onset time was 8 weeks (range: around 1 -30 weeks).
- ADA positive rate was 14.4% (30/208 patients) at the week 8 landmark in study IMCgp100-202.
- Median ADA titer was 8192. There were about 12.6% of patients who developed an ADA titer \ge8192.
L1873: ## Impact of immunogenicity on PK:
L1874: - Clearance increases by 32.9-fold in patients with high titer ADA (defined as titer&gt;8192). As a consequence, terminal half-life reduced from 6-7 hours to 10-14 minutes in this subgroup of patients. Relevant labeling of the basic characteristics of ADA and impact of immunogenicity on clearance was updated in section 6.2 and section 12.3 of the tebentafusp USPI.
L1875: ## Impact of immunogenicity on efficacy:
L1876: - An exploratory analysis of effect of ADA on outcomes of tebentafusp was conducted. For overall survival (OS), hazard ratio for the ADA 8 weeks' positive subgroup is 0.65 (95% CI: 0.36- 1.16) and hazard ratio for ADA 8 the weeks' negative subgroup is 0.44 (95% CI: 0.3, 0.64). The immunogenicity positive subgroup did not show significant reduced efficacy compared with the immunogenicity negative subgroup. However, the result needs to be interpreted with caution due to the limited number of patients (N=30) in the ADA 8 weeks' positive subgroup.
- An exploratory analysis of effect of high titer ADA on treatment effect of tebentafusp was also conducted. For overall survival (OS), hazard ratio for the high titer ADA 8 weeks' positive subgroup (titer\ge128) is 0.41 (95% CI: 0.35- 1.13) and hazard ratio for the ADA 8 weeks' low titer or negative subgroup is 0.48 (95% CI: 0.33, 0.69). The high titer ADA positive subgroup did not show significant reduced efficacy compared with the ADA negative or low titer subgroup. However, the result needs to be interpreted with caution due to the limited number of patients (N=11) in the high titer subgroup.
L1877: Please refer to section 19.5.4 for more details.
L1878: ## 19.5.1. Population PK Analysis
L1879: ## 19.5.1.1. The Applicant's Position:
L1880: Population Pharmacokinetic (PK) analysis was conducted across studies IMC-gp100-01, IMCgp100-102 and IMCgp100-202 to (1) characterize the PK properties of tebentafusp after IV infusion in melanoma patients and (2) to evaluate potential impact of patient demographics and clinical covariates on the PK exposure of tebentafusp. A two-compartment model with first-order elimination from the central compartment developed in patients with UM and non-UM was used to describe the PK profile. Tebentafusp Cmax was achieved immediately after the end of intravenous (IV) infusion (median 0.5 hours), with a geometric mean Cmax of 4063 pg/mL for Cycle 1 Day 1 (C1D1) (20 mcg dose) and 13040 pg/mL for C1D15 (68 mcg dose). The increase in geometric mean PK exposure (Cmax) of tebentafusp was approximately dose proportional between the 20 mcg dose administered on C1D1 and the 68 mcg dose administered on C1D15. Subsequent weekly doses of 68 mcg from Cycle 2 and beyond indicated no further accumulation of Tebentafusp. From the dose escalation data, the observed t_{1/2} was 6.8 to 7.5 hours.
L1881: Estimated glomerular filtration rate (eGFR) and high-titer anti-drug antibodies (ADA) were identified as statistically meaningful covariates of Tebentafusp clearance (CL). In the final population PK model, estimated tebentafusp CL slightly decreased in patients with moderate renal impairment (creatinine clearance [CrCL] ranging 30 to 59 mL/min). However, high interpatient variability (78%) was observed and CL values in renally impaired patients were essentially within the range observed in patients with normal renal function. No impact on safety or efficacy parameters was identified in patients with mild to moderate renal impairment and no dose adjustments are recommended. There is no information available in patients with severe renal impairment. Estimated CL considerably increased when patient's generated ADA titers greater than 1:8192. However, neither ADA onset or titer magnitude were associated with safety or efficacy and thus, monitoring of ADA is not necessary.

Objectives of PPK Analysis	To characterize the PK properties of tebentafusp in HLA A*02-positive melanoma patients
To evaluate the potential impact of demographic and clinical covariates on the PK exposure of tebentafusp
To evaluate the exposure-response relationship of clinical primary endpoints (eg, overall survival, tumor response)
To evaluate the exposure-response relationship of adverse events (eg, skin toxicity, hepatic toxicity, and Cytokine Release Syndrome [CRS]) and IL-6 levels
To evaluate the exposure-response of exploratory adverse events (eg, rash, hypo/hyper-pigmentation)

Study Included	
IMCgp100-01 - a Phase 1/2, open-label, dose finding study to assess the safety, tolerability, and efficacy of IMCgp100 in patients with advanced malignant melanoma.
IMCgp100-102 - a Phase 1/2, open-label, multi-center study of the safety and efficacy of IMCgp100 using the intra-patient escalation dosing regimen in patients with advanced uveal melanoma.
IMCgp100-202 - a Phase 3 randomized, open-label, multi-center study of the safety and efficacy of IMCgp100 compared with Investigator's choice in HLA-A*0201 positive patients with previously untreated advanced uveal melanoma.

L1911: The final PK model was evaluated using prediction corrected VPC (Bergstrand et al, 2011) to simulate 1000 replicated VPC datasets. The 95th percentile intervals across replicates were compared to corresponding summary statistics derived from observed data for composite VPC (Figure 43) and stratified by dose (Figure 44). The model predicted distribution overlap with the observed concentration well, indicating that the final PK model characterized the observed PK adequately.
L1912: **Figure 43. Composite Visual Predictive Check of Population PK Model (Log Scale [Run # 515])**
L1913: The blue circle represents the observed concentration. The solid and dashed lines represent the median and 2.5th and 97.5th percentiles of the observations. The shaded red and blue areas represent the 95% CI of the median and 2.5th and 97.5th percentiles predicted by the model, respectively.
L1914: Source: Population Pharmacokinetic Modeling Report and Exposure-Response Analysis for Tebentafusp, Figure 4
L1915: Figure 44. VPC of Final Population PK Model, Stratified by Dose Level
L1916: Blue circles represent observed concentrations. Solid and dashed lines represent the median and 2.5th and 97.5th percentiles of the observations, respectively. Shaded red and blue areas represent the 95% CI of median and 2.5th and 97.5th percentiles predicted by model, respectively.
L1917: Source: VPCs generated using final model code (5151.ct1)
L1918: The final model was developed by incorporating the effect of relevant covariates on key structural model parameters of the base model. Covariates were selected based on clinical judgment, mechanistic plausibility, and prior knowledge. PK parameter-covariate relationships were initially examined graphically. The model with a discrete covariate ADA effect (ADA titer \ge 8192) on CL and a continuous covariate of eGFR on CL were concluded to be the final covariate model (Run # 515).
L1919: **Figure 45. Correlation Matrix of Baseline Covariate Assessment-Continuous Covariates**
L1920: Source: Population Pharmacokinetic Modeling Report and Exposure Response Analysis for Tebentafusp, Appendix Figure 5
L1921: **Figure 46. Correlation Matrix of Baseline Covariate Assessment-Categorical Covariates**
L1922: Source: Population Pharmacokinetic Modeling Report and Exposure Response Analysis for Tebentafusp, Appendix Figure 6
L1923: Demographic covariates did not influence the inter-individual variability of the model based on graphical exploration. Therefore, clinical and physiologic covariates of interest, including renal function were added to the model in a stepwise fashion. Due to the molecular weight of tebentafusp, the effect of estimated glomerular filtration (eGFR) was investigated as a continuous covariate on CL. In the forwards addition of covariates, renal function exhibited the largest decrease in OFV and AIC.
L1924: **Figure 47. Impact of Renal Clearance by Renal Functional Status on Exposure After First or Third Dose**
L1925: Source: Analysis based on data "sdtab515"
L1926: The final model predicted a trend for faster tebentafusp clearance in ADA-positive patients than that of ADA-negative patients, but the distributions of IIV of CL overlapped between the 2 groups. The ADA effect on PK was further evaluated by stratification of patients with maximum ADA titers greater than or equal to the median titer of 1:8192. In this analysis, tebentafusp exposure was predicted to decrease in the presence of high ADA titers (\ge 8192) beyond the third dose.
L1927: **Figure 48.** Impact of ADA Titer \ge 8192 on Exposure.
L1928: Source: Analysis based on data "sdtab515"
L1929: ## 19.5.1.3. **The FDA's Assessment:**
L1930: The Applicant's population PK (PPK) model is generally acceptable with minor changes of the definition of immunogenicity. FDA's position is that ADA status should be treated as time-varying covariates. Please refer to section 19.5.4 for detail. Impact of ADA Titer \ge 8192 on exposure was re-estimated and the USPI was updated in section 12.3. The figures (Figure 15, Figure 48) visualizing the ADA effect on PK provided by the applicant treated ADA status or titer as a fixed factor; therefore, FDA does not agree with the presentation of the data.
L1931: Exposure comparison between hepatic impairment patients was requested by IR:
L1932: Exposure has been compared in patients with normal hepatic function (n=455) and patients with mild hepatic impairment (n=125) as measured by total bilirubin (TB) and aspartate aminotransferase (AST) (TB \le upper limit of normal (ULN) and AST &gt; ULN or TB &gt; 1 to 1.5x ULN and any AST). No clinically significant difference in the pharmacokinetics of tebentafusp were identified based on mild hepatic impairment (Figure 49).
L1933: Tebentafusp has not been studied in patients with moderate (TB &gt;1.5 to 3x ULN, any AST) to severe (TB &gt; 3 to 10x ULN, any AST) hepatic impairment.
L1934: Figure 49: Comparison of Clearance between Normal and Mild Hepatic Impairment Patients
L1935: Source: Response to FDA request for information received on 2021.12.08.
L1936: ## 19.5.2. Exposure-Response Analysis for Efficacy
L1937: ## 19.5.2.1. The Applicant's Position:
L1938: Exposure-response analysis revealed no trends between tebentafusp exposure and key efficacy endpoints over the dose range investigated (weekly intravenous [IV] infusion of 5 ng/kg to 68 mcg of tebentafusp). There was no clinically significant trend between PK exposure and overall survival (OS) or maximal change in tumor size from baseline, a surrogate of OS identified in Phase 2. There was no trend or association between ADA status and OS.

L1959: Overall, there were no clinically significant trends or associations exhibited between exposure and OS. The apparent trend can be explained by the fact that the lowest Cavg quartile is predominantly composed of patients dosed with lower concentrations of tebentafusp from study IMCgp100-01. Therefore, monitoring of tebentafusp exposure and dose adjustments are not necessary.

L1970: ## 19.5.2.3. **The FDA's Assessment:**

L1972: ## 19.5.3. **Exposure-Response Analysis for Safety**
L1973: ## 19.5.3.1. **The Applicant's Position:**
L1974: Exposure-response analysis revealed no trends between tebentafusp exposure and key safety endpoints over the dose range investigated (weekly intravenous [IV] infusion of 5 ng/kg to 68 mcg of tebentafusp). There was no association between PK exposure and acute skin reaction, rash, hypo/hyper-pigmentation, or hepatotoxicity. Cytokine release syndrome (CRS) is known to be dose dependent, and there was a trend for lower PK exposure in patients with no CRS. There was no trend between PK exposure and interleukin (IL) 6 concentration or either baseline or on-treatment lymphocyte counts.

L1981: Overall Clinical Relevance for ER	There was no clinically significant trend between PK exposure and overall survival (OS) or maximal change in tumor size from baseline, a surrogate of OS identified in Phase 2. Overall, there was no clinically significant trend or association between ADA status and key efficacy endpoints. There was no association between PK exposure and key on-treatment toxicities including: acute skin reactions, rash, hypo/hyperpigmentation or hepatotoxicity. CRS is known to be dose dependent, and there was a trend for lower PK exposure in patients with no CRS.

L2075: ## 19.5.3.3. The FDA's Assessment:
L2076: The Applicant's analyses were reasonable; however, exposure-response relationships for biologic products should be interpreted with caution due to the potential for post-hoc confounding effects.
L2077: ## 19.5.4. FDA's Overall Independent Assessment
L2078: ## Key Review Questions:
L2079: ## 1. What are the basic characteristics of tebentafusp immunogenicity?
L2080: The ADA positive rate was 28.6% (63/225 patients) in pivotal study IMCgp100-202 and 33.3% (48/144) in study IMCgp100-102. The ADA positive rate was 4% (3/78) in early-stage trial IMCgp100-01.
L2081: The time to median ADA onset was 8 weeks with a range from 1 - 30 weeks (Figure 68).
L2082: **Figure 68: Histogram of anti-drug antibody onset time (weeks)**
L2083: Source: Reviewer's independent analysis. Red line is the median onset time (8 weeks)
L2084: Tebentafusp ADA titer time course was shown in Figure 69 stratified by study and different dose levels.
L2085: **Figure 69: Tebentafusp Anti-Drug Antibody Titer Time Course Stratified by Studies**
L2086: Source: Reviewer's independent analysis. ADA titer was in natural logarithm scale.
L2087: ## 2. What is the impact of tebentafusp immunogenicity on tebentafusp pharmacokinetics?
L2088: ## Comparison of PK time course in ADA positive and ADA negative subgroups:
L2089: Tebentafusp drug concentrations over time were compared in the ADA positive subgroup (represented by red dots and dashed line) with the ADA negative subgroup (represented by blue solid box and solid line) in Figure 70. A difference could be visualized in Cmax of each infusion cycle as there was no concentration detectable at the end of each infusion cycle.
L2090: **Figure 70: Comparison of PK time course in ADA positive and ADA negative subgroups:**
L2091: Source: Clinical Pharm Team Reviewer's Independent Analysis.
L2092: ## Population PK Analysis:
L2093: Since ADA titer is a time-varying covariate, FDA requested the applicant to re-define MAXTITFL as follows:
L2094: - When "ADATITER" \ge8129, "MAXTITFL" was defined as 1.
- Otherwise "MAXTITFL" was defined as "."
L2095: With the new dataset "nonmen200.csv" and the final structure model (run 515), the newly estimated high titer ADA (&gt;8192) increased tebentafusp clearance 32.9-fold. And this value was updated in section 12.3 of the tebentafusp USPI.
L2096: ## 3. What is the impact of tebentafusp immunogenicity on efficacy?
L2097: An exploratory analysis of the effect of ADA on treatment outcomes of tebentafusp was conducted (Figure 71: left). Patients in both the tebentafusp arm and the control arm who had an event or were censored less than 8 weeks were removed. The new ADA status grouped based on ADA status at 8 weeks were listed as follows:
L2098: ADA 8 weeks status	N (%)
ADA+ 8 weeks	30/208 (14.4%)
ADA- 8 weeks	178/208 (85.6%)
L2099: Based on cox regression analysis, for overall survival (OS), the hazard ratio for the ADA 8 weeks' positive subgroup is 0.65 (95% CI: 0.36- 1.16) and hazard ratio for the ADA 8 weeks' negative subgroup is 0.44 (95% CI: 0.3, 0.64) (Table 79). The ADA positive subgroup did not show significant reduced efficacy compared with the ADA negative subgroup. However, these results need to be interpreted with caution due to the limited number of patients (N=30) in the ADA 8 weeks' positive subgroup.
L2100: To explore the impact of high titer ADA on efficacy, an exploratory analysis of the effect of high titer ADA on treatment outcomes of tebentafusp was conducted (Figure 71, right). Based on cox regression model, for overall survival (OS), the hazard ratio for high titer ADA 8 weeks' positive subgroup (titer\ge128) is 0.41 (95% CI: 0.35- 1.13) and hazard ratio for ADA 8 weeks' low titer or negative subgroup is 0.48 (95% CI: 0.33, 0.69) (Table 79). The cut-off of titer 128 was the median ADA titer at week 8. The high titer ADA positive subgroup did not show significant reduced efficacy compared with the ADA negative or low titer subgroup. However, these results need to be interpreted with caution due to the limited number of patients (N=11) in the high titer subgroup.

Green line represents ADA 8 weeks negative patients (overall negative in the left figure and titer&lt;128 in the right figure). Gray line represents control group.

L2106: ## 19.5.5. Summary of Bioanalytical Method
L2107: The Meso Scale Discovery (MSD) method was used to determine tebentafusp in human serum using HLA-A2 antigen capture in Study IMCgp100-01, Study IMCgp100-102 and IMCgp100-202. This method employs an indirect sandwich immunoassay format, with biotinylated HLA-A2 gp100 antigen used to capture the tebentafusp monoclonal TCR. After incubation with analyte, a rabbit anti-gp100 TCR antibody step is used followed by detection with a SULFO-TAG conjugated goat anti-rabbit antibody. The lower limit of quantification (LLOQ) of the method is 25 pg/mL and the upper limit of quantification (ULOQ) is 5,000 pg/mL. This method was developed and validated at (b) (4).
L2108: Analytical Method (b) (4)/007/V10 was validated over the concentration range 25 to 5,000 pg/mL in undiluted serum samples. The concentration levels of tebentafusp (Lot 00644, 0.5 mg/mL concentration) were as follows: 25 (LLOQ), 125 (Low), 600 (Medium), 3,200 (High), 5,000 (ULOQ) and 50,000 (dilution) pg/mL. The dilution quality control was diluted 100-fold with control human serum prior to analysis and the data confirmed linearity of dilution into the working range of the assay with up to 100-fold dilution in serum.
L2109: The intra-batch precision was less than or equal to 14.0% and the intra-run bias was between -13.6% and 3.0%. The inter-batch precision was less than or equal to 13.7% and the inter-batch bias was between -9.4% and 5.6%. The total error of the method was less than, or equal to 21.1% for the intra-batch data and less than or equal to 21.3% for the inter-batch data. The target LLOQ in this validation was 25 pg/mL; precision and bias at this level were found to be acceptable.
L2110: The long-term stability of tebentafusp (Lot 00644, 0.5 mg/mL concentration) when spiked into human serum and stored at -80°C was successfully assessed and established using the analytical method previously validated at YBS. Stability was assessed after approximately 6, 12, and 24 months. In addition, the stability of tebentafusp in working solution (assay buffer) when stored at -80°C was investigated after approximately 6 and 12 months of storage in polypropylene containers. Tebentafusp was found to be stable in human serum stored at -80°C for up to 24 months and in working solution when stored at -80°C for up to 373 days.

L2171: Bar chart showing the percentage of patients receiving Tebentafusp infusion who experienced Grade 2-4 Cytokine Release Syndrome (CRS) by infusion number (Week). The Y-axis is '% of Patients with CRS' (0.0 to 80.0). The X-axis is 'Infusion Number' (1 to 19). The percentage of patients with CRS is highest after the first infusion (approx. 70%), drops significantly after the second infusion (approx. 55%), and continues to decrease sharply, reaching near 0% by infusion number 10. After infusion number 10, the percentage remains near 0% up to infusion number 19.

L2176: Composite Visual Predictive Check (VPC) plot for the Population PK Model (Log Scale, Run # 515). The Y-axis is 'Observations' (log scale, ranging from 10 to 10000) and the X-axis is 'Time' (ranging from 0 to 1000). Observed concentrations are represented by blue circles. The median and 2.5th and 97.5th percentiles of the observations are shown by solid and dashed red lines. The 95% Confidence Interval (CI) of the median and 2.5th and 97.5th percentiles predicted by the model are shown by shaded red and blue areas, respectively. The plot shows good overlap between the observed data and the predicted distribution, indicating the model adequately characterizes the observed PK.
L2177: Figure 44 displays four Visual Predictive Checks (VPCs) for the final population PK model of Kimtrak (tebentafusp), stratified by dose level. The plots compare observed concentrations (blue circles) against model predictions (solid and dashed lines representing median and percentiles, and shaded areas representing 95% CI).
The Y-axis for all plots is Tebentafusp Concentration (pg/mL) on a logarithmic scale (100 to 100000). The X-axis is Time (days).
The four panels are:
All Other Doses: Shows a wide range of time points (0 to 1000 days) and concentrations. The model predicts a rapid initial peak followed by a decline, with observed concentrations generally falling within the predicted 95% CI.
20 mcg Dose: Shows time points up to 500 days. The model predicts a rapid peak and subsequent decline, with observed concentrations closely matching the predicted median and percentiles.
30mcg Dose: Shows time points up to 40 days. The model predicts a rapid peak and subsequent decline, with observed concentrations closely matching the predicted median and percentiles.
68mcg Dose: Shows time points up to 1000 days. The model predicts a rapid peak and subsequent decline, with observed concentrations generally falling within the predicted 95% CI.
